<p><b>Belize</b> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="&#39;b&#39; in &#39;buy&#39;">b</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;l&#39; in &#39;lie&#39;">l</span><span title="/iː/: &#39;ee&#39; in &#39;fleece&#39;">iː</span><span title="&#39;z&#39; in &#39;zoom&#39;">z</span></span>/</a></span>&#32;<span class="nowrap" style="font-size:85%">(<span class="unicode haudio"><span class="fn"><span style="white-space:nowrap;margin-right:.25em;"><a href="/wiki/File:En-us-Belize.ogg" title="About this sound"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/11px-Loudspeaker.svg.png" decoding="async" width="11" height="11" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/17px-Loudspeaker.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8a/Loudspeaker.svg/22px-Loudspeaker.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="20" data-file-height="20" /></a></span><a href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/c/c5/En-us-Belize.ogg" class="internal" title="En-us-Belize.ogg">listen</a></span></span>)</span></span>; <a href="/wiki/Belize_Kriol_English_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Belize Kriol English language">Belize Kriol English</a>: <i lang="bzj">Bileez</i>) is a country on the north-eastern coast of <a href="/wiki/Central_America" title="Central America">Central America</a>. It is bordered by <a href="/wiki/Mexico" title="Mexico">Mexico</a> to the north, the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Sea" title="Caribbean Sea">Caribbean Sea</a> to the east, and <a href="/wiki/Guatemala" title="Guatemala">Guatemala</a> to the west and south. It also shares a water boundary with <a href="/wiki/Honduras" title="Honduras">Honduras</a> to the southeast. It has an area of 22,970 square kilometres (8,867&#160;sq&#160;mi) and a population of 441,471 (2022).<sup id="cite_ref-est_7-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-est-7">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> Its mainland is about 290&#160;km (180&#160;mi) long and 110&#160;km (68&#160;mi) wide. It is the least populated and <a href="/wiki/Population_density" title="Population density">least densely populated country</a> in Central America. Its population growth rate of 1.87% per year (2018 estimate) is the second-highest in the region and one of the <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_population_growth_rate" title="List of countries by population growth rate">highest in the Western Hemisphere</a>. Its <a href="/wiki/Capital_city" title="Capital city">capital</a> is <a href="/wiki/Belmopan" title="Belmopan">Belmopan</a>, and its largest city is the namesake city of <a href="/wiki/Belize_City" title="Belize City">Belize City</a>. Belize is often thought of as a Caribbean country in Central America because it has a history similar to that of English-speaking Caribbean nations. Belize's institutions and official language reflect its history as a British colony. 
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Maya_civilization" title="Maya civilization">Maya civilization</a> spread into the area of Belize between 1500&#160;BC and AD 300 and flourished until about 1200.<sup id="cite_ref-cs_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cs-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> European contact began in 1492 when <a href="/wiki/Christopher_Columbus" title="Christopher Columbus">Christopher Columbus</a> sailed along the <a href="/wiki/Gulf_of_Honduras" title="Gulf of Honduras">Gulf of Honduras</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> European exploration was begun by English settlers in 1638. <a href="/wiki/Spanish_Empire" title="Spanish Empire">Spain</a> and <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Great_Britain" title="Kingdom of Great Britain">Britain</a> both laid claim to the land until Britain defeated the Spanish in the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_St._George%27s_Caye" title="Battle of St. George&#39;s Caye">Battle of St. George's Caye</a> (1798).<sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup> In 1840 it became a British colony known as <a href="/wiki/British_Honduras" title="British Honduras">British Honduras</a>, and a <a href="/wiki/Crown_colony" title="Crown colony">Crown colony</a> in 1862. Belize achieved its independence from the United Kingdom on 21 September 1981.<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> It is the only mainland Central American country which is a <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_realm" title="Commonwealth realm">Commonwealth realm</a>, with King <a href="/wiki/Charles_III" title="Charles III">Charles III</a> as <a href="/wiki/Monarchy_of_Belize" title="Monarchy of Belize">its monarch</a> and <a href="/wiki/Head_of_state" title="Head of state">head of state</a>, represented by a <a href="/wiki/Governor-General_of_Belize" title="Governor-General of Belize">governor-general</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Belize has a diverse society composed of many cultures and languages, reflecting its rich history. It is the only Central American country where English is the official language, while <a href="/wiki/Belizean_Creole" title="Belizean Creole">Belizean Creole</a> is the most widely spoken dialect. <a href="/wiki/Belizean_Spanish" title="Belizean Spanish">Spanish</a> is the second-most-commonly-spoken language, followed by the <a href="/wiki/Mayan_languages" title="Mayan languages">Mayan languages</a>, <a href="/wiki/German_dialects" title="German dialects">German dialects</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Garifuna_language" title="Garifuna language">Garifuna</a>. Over half the population is <a href="/wiki/Multilingual" class="mw-redirect" title="Multilingual">multilingual</a>, due to the diverse linguistic backgrounds of the population. It is known for its <a href="/wiki/September_Celebrations" title="September Celebrations">September Celebrations</a>, its extensive barrier reef <a href="/wiki/Coral_reef" title="Coral reef">coral reefs</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Punta" title="Punta">punta music</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-ryan_18-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ryan-18">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup> 
</p><p>Belize's abundance of terrestrial and marine species and its diversity of ecosystems give it a key place in the globally significant <a href="/wiki/Mesoamerican_Biological_Corridor" title="Mesoamerican Biological Corridor">Mesoamerican Biological Corridor</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> It is considered a <a href="/wiki/Central_America" title="Central America">Central American</a> and <a href="/wiki/Caribbean" title="Caribbean">Caribbean</a> nation with strong ties to both the American and Caribbean regions.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> It is a member of the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Community" title="Caribbean Community">Caribbean Community</a> (CARICOM), the <a href="/wiki/Community_of_Latin_American_and_Caribbean_States" title="Community of Latin American and Caribbean States">Community of Latin American and Caribbean States</a> (CELAC), and the <a href="/wiki/Central_American_Integration_System" title="Central American Integration System">Central American Integration System</a> (SICA), the only country to hold full membership in all three regional organizations.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Name">Name</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=1" title="Edit section: Name">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<p>The earliest known record of the name "Belize" appears in the journal of the <a href="/wiki/Dominican_Order" title="Dominican Order">Dominican</a> priest Fray José Delgado, dating to 1677.<sup id="cite_ref-Twigg_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Twigg-21">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> Delgado recorded the names of three major rivers that he crossed while travelling north along the Caribbean coast: Rio Soyte, Rio Kibum, and Rio Balis. The names of these waterways, which correspond to the <a href="/wiki/Sittee_River" title="Sittee River">Sittee River</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sibun_River" title="Sibun River">Sibun River</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Belize_River" title="Belize River">Belize River</a>, were provided to Delgado by his translator.<sup id="cite_ref-Twigg_21-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Twigg-21">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> It has been proposed that Delgado's "Balis" was actually the Mayan word <i>belix</i> (or <i>belize</i>), meaning "muddy-watered".<sup id="cite_ref-Twigg_21-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Twigg-21">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup> More recently, it has been proposed that the name comes from the Mayan phrase "bel Itza", meaning "the road to <a href="/wiki/Chichen_Itza" title="Chichen Itza">Itza</a>".<sup id="cite_ref-Restall_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Restall-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the 1820s, the Creole elite of Belize invented the legend that the toponym Belize derived from the Spanish pronunciation of the name of a Scottish <a href="/wiki/Buccaneer" title="Buccaneer">buccaneer</a>, <a href="/wiki/Peter_Wallace_(buccaneer)" title="Peter Wallace (buccaneer)">Peter Wallace</a>, who established a settlement at the mouth of the Belize River in 1638.<sup id="cite_ref-brit1893_23-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-brit1893-23">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup> There is no proof that buccaneers settled in this area and the very existence of Wallace is considered a myth.<sup id="cite_ref-Twigg_21-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Twigg-21">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Restall_22-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Restall-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup> Writers and historians have suggested several other possible etymologies, including postulated French and African origins.<sup id="cite_ref-Twigg_21-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Twigg-21">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=2" title="Edit section: History">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Belize" title="History of Belize">History of Belize</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_history">Early history</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=3" title="Edit section: Early history">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Mayamap.png" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/Mayamap.png/220px-Mayamap.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="162" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/Mayamap.png/330px-Mayamap.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/Mayamap.png/440px-Mayamap.png 2x" data-file-width="1074" data-file-height="789" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Mayamap.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Extent of the Maya civilization</div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Maya_civilization" title="Maya civilization">Maya civilization</a> emerged at least three millennia ago in the lowland area of the <a href="/wiki/Yucat%C3%A1n_Peninsula" title="Yucatán Peninsula">Yucatán Peninsula</a> and the highlands to the south, in the area of present-day southeastern Mexico, Belize, <a href="/wiki/Guatemala" title="Guatemala">Guatemala</a>, and western <a href="/wiki/Honduras" title="Honduras">Honduras</a>. Many aspects of this culture persist in the area, despite nearly 500 years of European domination. Prior to about 2500 BC, some <a href="/wiki/Hunter-gatherer" title="Hunter-gatherer">hunting and foraging</a> bands settled in small farming villages; they domesticated crops such as corn, beans, <a href="/wiki/Cucurbita" title="Cucurbita">squash</a>, and chili peppers.
</p><p>A profusion of languages and subcultures developed within the Maya core culture. Between about 2500 BC and 250 AD, the basic institutions of Maya civilization emerged.<sup id="cite_ref-cs_12-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cs-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Caracolfacup_o.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/Caracolfacup_o.jpg/220px-Caracolfacup_o.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/Caracolfacup_o.jpg/330px-Caracolfacup_o.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/Caracolfacup_o.jpg/440px-Caracolfacup_o.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3072" data-file-height="2304" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Caracolfacup_o.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>"Caana" at <a href="/wiki/Caracol" title="Caracol">Caracol</a></div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Xunantunich09.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/73/Xunantunich09.jpg/220px-Xunantunich09.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="144" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/73/Xunantunich09.jpg/330px-Xunantunich09.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/73/Xunantunich09.jpg/440px-Xunantunich09.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1983" data-file-height="1302" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Xunantunich09.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>"El Castillo" at <a href="/wiki/Xunantunich" title="Xunantunich">Xunantunich</a></div></div></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Maya_civilization">Maya civilization</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=4" title="Edit section: Maya civilization">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Pre-Columbian_Belize" title="Pre-Columbian Belize">Pre-Columbian Belize</a></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Maya_civilization" title="Maya civilization">Maya civilization</a> spread across the territory of present-day Belize around 1500<span class="nowrap">&#160;</span>BC, and flourished until about <span class="nowrap">&#160;</span>900 AD. The recorded history of the middle and southern regions focuses on <a href="/wiki/Caracol" title="Caracol">Caracol</a>, an urban political centre that may have supported over 140,000 people.<sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup> North of the <a href="/wiki/Maya_Mountains" title="Maya Mountains">Maya Mountains</a>, the most important political centre was <a href="/wiki/Lamanai" title="Lamanai">Lamanai</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> In the late <a href="/wiki/Mesoamerican_chronology" title="Mesoamerican chronology">Classic Era</a> of Maya civilization (600–1000<span class="nowrap">&#160;</span>AD), an estimated 400,000 to 1,000,000 people inhabited the area of present-day Belize.<sup id="cite_ref-cs_12-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cs-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>When Spanish explorers arrived in the 16th century, the area of present-day Belize included three distinct Maya territories:<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<ul><li><a href="/wiki/Chetumal_Province" title="Chetumal Province">Chetumal province</a>, which encompassed the area around <a href="/wiki/Corozal_Bay" title="Corozal Bay">Corozal Bay</a></li>
<li><a href="/wiki/Dzuluinicob" title="Dzuluinicob">Dzuluinicob</a> province,<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup> which encompassed the area between the <a href="/wiki/New_River_(Belize)" title="New River (Belize)">New River</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Sibun_River" title="Sibun River">Sibun River</a>, west to <a href="/wiki/Tipu,_Belize" title="Tipu, Belize">Tipu</a></li>
<li>a southern territory controlled by the <a href="/wiki/Manche_Ch%27ol" class="mw-redirect" title="Manche Ch&#39;ol">Manche Ch'ol</a> Maya, encompassing the area between the <a href="/wiki/Monkey_River" title="Monkey River">Monkey River</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Sarstoon_River" title="Sarstoon River">Sarstoon River</a>.</li></ul>
<h3><span id="Early_colonial_period_.281506.E2.80.931862.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Early_colonial_period_(1506–1862)">Early colonial period (1506–1862)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=5" title="Edit section: Early colonial period (1506–1862)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Belize_(1506%E2%80%931862)" title="History of Belize (1506–1862)">History of Belize (1506–1862)</a> and <a href="/wiki/Colony_of_Jamaica" title="Colony of Jamaica">Colony of Jamaica</a></div>
<p>Spanish <a href="/wiki/Conquistador" title="Conquistador">conquistadors</a> explored the land and declared it part of the <a href="/wiki/Spanish_Empire" title="Spanish Empire">Spanish Empire</a>, but they failed to settle the territory because of its lack of resources and the hostile tribes of the Yucatán.
</p><p>English pirates sporadically visited the coast of what is now Belize, seeking a sheltered region from which they could attack Spanish ships <i>(see <a href="/wiki/English_settlement_in_Belize" class="mw-redirect" title="English settlement in Belize">English settlement in Belize</a>)</i> and cut <a href="/wiki/Logwood" class="mw-redirect" title="Logwood">logwood</a> (<i>Haematoxylum campechianum</i>) trees. The first British permanent settlement was founded around 1716, nearly 200 years after the Spanish had initially arrived, in what became the <a href="/wiki/Belize_District" title="Belize District">Belize District</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-Johnson2003_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Johnson2003-30">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> and during the 18th century, established a system using <a href="/wiki/Atlantic_slave_trade" title="Atlantic slave trade">enslaved Africans</a> to cut logwood trees. This yielded a valuable fixing agent for clothing dyes,<sup id="cite_ref-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-31">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> and was one of the first ways to achieve a <a href="/wiki/Natural_dye#The_rise_of_formal_black" title="Natural dye">fast black</a> before the advent of artificial dyes. The Spanish granted the British settlers the right to occupy the area and cut logwood in exchange for their help suppressing piracy.<sup id="cite_ref-cs_12-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cs-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:St._George%27s_Cay_Day_Official_Holiday.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/03/St._George%27s_Cay_Day_Official_Holiday.jpg/220px-St._George%27s_Cay_Day_Official_Holiday.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="170" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/03/St._George%27s_Cay_Day_Official_Holiday.jpg/330px-St._George%27s_Cay_Day_Official_Holiday.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/03/St._George%27s_Cay_Day_Official_Holiday.jpg/440px-St._George%27s_Cay_Day_Official_Holiday.jpg 2x" data-file-width="885" data-file-height="682" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:St._George%27s_Cay_Day_Official_Holiday.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>An excerpt from the 1898 Gazette that declared 10 September an official holiday, part of the efforts of the Centennial Committee</div></div></div>
<p>The British first appointed a superintendent over the Belize area in 1786. Before then the British government had not recognized the settlement as a colony for fear of provoking a <a href="/wiki/Spanish_Empire" title="Spanish Empire">Spanish</a> attack. The delay in government oversight allowed the settlers to establish their own laws and forms of government. During this period, a few successful settlers gained control of the local legislature, known as the Public Meeting, as well as of most of the settlement's land and timber.
</p><p>Throughout the 18th century, the Spanish attacked Belize every time war broke out with Britain. The <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_St._George%27s_Caye" title="Battle of St. George&#39;s Caye">Battle of St. George's Caye</a> was the last of such military engagements, in 1798, between a Spanish fleet and a small force of <a href="/wiki/Baymen" title="Baymen">Baymen</a> and their slaves. From 3 to 5 September, the Spaniards tried to force their way through Montego Caye shoal, but were blocked by defenders. Spain's last attempt occurred on 10 September, when the Baymen repelled the Spanish fleet in a short engagement with no known casualties on either side. The anniversary of the battle has been declared a national holiday in Belize and is celebrated to commemorate the "first Belizeans" and the defence of their territory taken from the Spanish empire.<sup id="cite_ref-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-32">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="As_part_of_the_British_Empire_.281862.E2.80.931981.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="As_part_of_the_British_Empire_(1862–1981)">As part of the British Empire (1862–1981)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=6" title="Edit section: As part of the British Empire (1862–1981)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/British_Honduras" title="British Honduras">British Honduras</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Flag_of_British_Honduras_(1870%E2%80%931919).svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/de/Flag_of_British_Honduras_%281870%E2%80%931919%29.svg/220px-Flag_of_British_Honduras_%281870%E2%80%931919%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="110" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/de/Flag_of_British_Honduras_%281870%E2%80%931919%29.svg/330px-Flag_of_British_Honduras_%281870%E2%80%931919%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/de/Flag_of_British_Honduras_%281870%E2%80%931919%29.svg/440px-Flag_of_British_Honduras_%281870%E2%80%931919%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1000" data-file-height="500" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Flag_of_British_Honduras_(1870%E2%80%931919).svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Colonial flag of British Honduras, 1870–1919</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Flag_of_British_Honduras_(1919%E2%80%931981).svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f4/Flag_of_British_Honduras_%281919%E2%80%931981%29.svg/220px-Flag_of_British_Honduras_%281919%E2%80%931981%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="110" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f4/Flag_of_British_Honduras_%281919%E2%80%931981%29.svg/330px-Flag_of_British_Honduras_%281919%E2%80%931981%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f4/Flag_of_British_Honduras_%281919%E2%80%931981%29.svg/440px-Flag_of_British_Honduras_%281919%E2%80%931981%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1000" data-file-height="500" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Flag_of_British_Honduras_(1919%E2%80%931981).svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Colonial flag of British Honduras, 1919–1981</div></div></div>
<p>In the early 19th century, the British sought to reform the settlers, threatening to suspend the Public Meeting unless it observed the government's instructions to eliminate slavery outright. After a generation of wrangling, slavery was <a href="/wiki/Slavery_Abolition_Act_1833" title="Slavery Abolition Act 1833">abolished</a> in the <a href="/wiki/British_Empire" title="British Empire">British Empire</a> in 1833.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup> As a result of their enslaved Africans' abilities in the work of <a href="/wiki/Mahogany" title="Mahogany">mahogany</a> extraction, owners in <a href="/wiki/British_Honduras" title="British Honduras">British Honduras</a> were compensated at £53.69 per enslaved African on average, the highest amount paid in any British territory. This was a form of reparation that was not given to the enslaved Africans at the time, nor since.<sup id="cite_ref-Johnson2003_30-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Johnson2003-30">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The end of slavery did little to change the formerly enslaved Africans' working conditions if they stayed at their trade. A series of institutions restricted the ability of emancipated African individuals to buy land, in a debt-peonage system. Former "extra special" mahogany or logwood cutters undergirded the early ascription of the capacities (and consequently the limitations) of people of African descent in the colony. Because a small elite controlled the settlement's land and commerce, formerly enslaved Africans had little choice but to continue to work in timber cutting.<sup id="cite_ref-Johnson2003_30-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Johnson2003-30">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1836, after the <a href="/wiki/Spanish_American_wars_of_independence" title="Spanish American wars of independence">emancipation of Central America from Spanish rule</a>, the British claimed the right to administer the region. In 1862, the United Kingdom formally declared it a <a href="/wiki/British_Crown_Colony" class="mw-redirect" title="British Crown Colony">British Crown Colony</a>, subordinate to Jamaica, and named it British Honduras.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup> Since 1854, the richest inhabitants elected an assembly of notables by censal vote, which was replaced by a legislative council appointed by the British monarchy.<sup id="cite_ref-clio_35-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-clio-35">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As a colony, Belize began to attract British investors. Among the British firms that dominated the colony in the late 19th century was the Belize Estate and Produce Company, which eventually acquired half of all privately held land and eventually eliminated <a href="/wiki/Peonage" class="mw-redirect" title="Peonage">peonage</a>. Belize Estate's influence accounts in part for the colony's reliance on the mahogany trade throughout the rest of the 19th century and the first half of the 20th century.
</p>
<div class="thumb tnone" style="margin-left:auto;margin-right:auto;overflow:hidden;width:auto;max-width:2008px"><div class="thumbinner"><div class="noresize" style="overflow:auto"><a href="/wiki/File:British_Honduras_1914.jpg" class="image" title="Panoramic view of Belize City, c. 1914"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/61/British_Honduras_1914.jpg/2000px-British_Honduras_1914.jpg" decoding="async" width="2000" height="136" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/61/British_Honduras_1914.jpg/3000px-British_Honduras_1914.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/61/British_Honduras_1914.jpg/4000px-British_Honduras_1914.jpg 2x" data-file-width="6550" data-file-height="444" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption" style="text-align:center"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:British_Honduras_1914.jpg" title="File:British Honduras 1914.jpg"> </a></div>Panoramic view of Belize City, <abbr title="circa">c.</abbr><span style="white-space:nowrap;">&#8201;1914</span></div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Great_Depression" title="Great Depression">Great Depression</a> of the 1930s caused a near-collapse of the colony's economy as British demand for timber plummeted. The effects of widespread unemployment were worsened by a <a href="/wiki/1931_Belize_hurricane" class="mw-redirect" title="1931 Belize hurricane">devastating hurricane</a> that struck the colony in 1931. Perceptions of the government's relief effort as inadequate were aggravated by its refusal to legalize labour unions or introduce a minimum wage. Economic conditions improved during <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a>, as many Belizean men entered the armed forces or otherwise contributed to the war effort.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:British_Honduras_1962_Hurricane_Hattie_stamp.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/48/British_Honduras_1962_Hurricane_Hattie_stamp.jpg/220px-British_Honduras_1962_Hurricane_Hattie_stamp.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="148" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/48/British_Honduras_1962_Hurricane_Hattie_stamp.jpg/330px-British_Honduras_1962_Hurricane_Hattie_stamp.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/48/British_Honduras_1962_Hurricane_Hattie_stamp.jpg/440px-British_Honduras_1962_Hurricane_Hattie_stamp.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1280" data-file-height="859" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:British_Honduras_1962_Hurricane_Hattie_stamp.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A British Honduras postage stamp overprinted in 1962 to mark <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Hattie" title="Hurricane Hattie">Hurricane Hattie</a></div></div></div>
<p>Following the war, the colony's economy stagnated. Britain's decision to <a href="/wiki/Devalue" class="mw-redirect" title="Devalue">devalue</a> the British Honduras dollar in 1949 worsened economic conditions and led to the creation of the People's Committee, which demanded independence. The People's Committee's successor, the <a href="/wiki/People%27s_United_Party" title="People&#39;s United Party">People's United Party</a> (PUP), sought constitutional reforms that expanded voting rights to all adults. The first election under <a href="/wiki/Universal_suffrage" title="Universal suffrage">universal suffrage</a> was held <a href="/wiki/British_Honduras_general_election,_1954" class="mw-redirect" title="British Honduras general election, 1954">in 1954</a> and was decisively won by the PUP, beginning a three-decade period in which the PUP dominated the country's politics. Pro-independence activist <a href="/wiki/George_Cadle_Price" title="George Cadle Price">George Cadle Price</a> became PUP's leader in 1956 and the effective head of government in 1961, a post he would hold under various titles until 1984.
</p><p>Under a new constitution, Britain granted British Honduras self-government in 1964. On 1 June 1973, British Honduras was officially renamed Belize.<sup id="cite_ref-caricom_36-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-caricom-36">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> Progress toward independence was hampered by a <a href="/wiki/Belizean%E2%80%93Guatemalan_territorial_dispute" title="Belizean–Guatemalan territorial dispute">Guatemalan claim to sovereignty over Belizean territory</a>.
</p>
<h3><span id="Independent_Belize_.28since_1981.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Independent_Belize_(since_1981)">Independent Belize (since 1981)</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=7" title="Edit section: Independent Belize (since 1981)">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Monarchy_of_Belize" title="Monarchy of Belize">Monarchy of Belize</a></div>
<p>Belize was granted independence on 21 September 1981. <a href="/wiki/Guatemala" title="Guatemala">Guatemala</a> refused to recognize the new nation because of its longstanding territorial dispute with the British colony, claiming that Belize belonged to Guatemala. About 1,500 British troops remained in Belize to deter any possible incursions.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>With <a href="/wiki/George_Cadle_Price" title="George Cadle Price">George Cadle Price</a> at the helm, the PUP won all national elections <a href="/wiki/Belizean_general_election,_1984" class="mw-redirect" title="Belizean general election, 1984">until 1984</a>. In that election, the first national election after independence, the PUP was defeated by the <a href="/wiki/United_Democratic_Party_(Belize)" title="United Democratic Party (Belize)">United Democratic Party</a> (UDP). UDP leader <a href="/wiki/Manuel_Esquivel" title="Manuel Esquivel">Manuel Esquivel</a> replaced Price as prime minister, with Price himself unexpectedly losing his own <a href="/wiki/House_of_Representatives_(Belize)" title="House of Representatives (Belize)">House</a> seat to a UDP challenger. The PUP under Price returned to power after elections <a href="/wiki/Belizean_general_election,_1989" class="mw-redirect" title="Belizean general election, 1989">in 1989</a>. The following year the United Kingdom announced that it would end its military involvement in Belize, and the RAF Harrier detachment was withdrawn the same year, having remained stationed in the country continuously since its deployment had become permanent there in 1980. British soldiers were withdrawn in 1994, but the United Kingdom left behind a military training unit to assist with the newly created <a href="/wiki/Belize_Defence_Force" title="Belize Defence Force">Belize Defence Force</a>.
</p><p>The UDP regained power in the <a href="/wiki/Belizean_general_election,_1993" class="mw-redirect" title="Belizean general election, 1993">1993 national election</a>, and Esquivel became prime minister for a second time. Soon afterwards, Esquivel announced the suspension of a pact reached with Guatemala during Price's tenure, claiming Price had made too many concessions to gain Guatemalan recognition. The pact may have curtailed the 130-year-old border dispute between the two countries. Border tensions continued into the early 2000s, although the two countries cooperated in other areas.
</p><p>In 1996, the <a href="/wiki/Belize_Barrier_Reef" title="Belize Barrier Reef">Belize Barrier Reef</a>, one of the Western Hemisphere's most pristine ecosystems, was designated as a <a href="/wiki/UNESCO_World_Heritage_Site" class="mw-redirect" title="UNESCO World Heritage Site">UNESCO World Heritage Site</a>.
</p><p>The PUP won a landslide victory in the 1998 national elections, and PUP leader <a href="/wiki/Said_Musa" title="Said Musa">Said Musa</a> was sworn in as prime minister. In the 2003 elections the PUP maintained its majority, and Musa continued as prime minister. He pledged to improve conditions in the underdeveloped and largely inaccessible southern part of Belize.
</p><p>In 2005, Belize was the site of <a href="/wiki/2005_Belize_unrest" title="2005 Belize unrest">unrest</a> caused by discontent with the PUP government, including tax increases in the national budget. On 8 February 2008, <a href="/wiki/Dean_Barrow" title="Dean Barrow">Dean Barrow</a> was sworn in as prime minister after his <a href="/wiki/United_Democratic_Party_(Belize)" title="United Democratic Party (Belize)">UDP</a> won <a href="/wiki/Belizean_general_election,_2008" class="mw-redirect" title="Belizean general election, 2008">a landslide victory</a> in general elections. Barrow and the UDP were re-elected <a href="/wiki/Belizean_general_and_local_elections,_2012" class="mw-redirect" title="Belizean general and local elections, 2012">in 2012</a> with a considerably smaller majority. Barrow led the UDP to a third consecutive general election victory in November 2015, increasing the party's number of seats from 17 to 19. He said the election would be his last as party leader and preparations are under way for the party to elect his successor.
</p><p>On 11 November 2020, the <a href="/wiki/People%27s_United_Party" title="People&#39;s United Party">People's United Party</a> (PUP), led by <a href="/wiki/Johnny_Brice%C3%B1o" title="Johnny Briceño">Johnny Briceño</a>, defeated the <a href="/wiki/United_Democratic_Party_(Belize)" title="United Democratic Party (Belize)">United Democratic Party</a> (UDP) for the first time since 2003, having won 26 seats out of 31 to form the new government of Belize. Briceño took office as Prime Minister on 12 November.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Government_and_politics">Government and politics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=8" title="Edit section: Government and politics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Belize" title="Politics of Belize">Politics of Belize</a></div><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Belize" title="Government of Belize">Government of Belize</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Belmopan_Parliament.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Belmopan_Parliament.jpg/220px-Belmopan_Parliament.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="163" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Belmopan_Parliament.jpg/330px-Belmopan_Parliament.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Belmopan_Parliament.jpg/440px-Belmopan_Parliament.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3213" data-file-height="2379" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Belmopan_Parliament.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/National_Assembly_(Belize)" title="National Assembly (Belize)">National Assembly</a> in <a href="/wiki/Belmopan" title="Belmopan">Belmopan</a></div></div></div>
<p>Belize is a <a href="/wiki/Parliamentary_system" title="Parliamentary system">parliamentary</a> <a href="/wiki/Constitutional_monarchy" title="Constitutional monarchy">constitutional monarchy</a>. The structure of government is based on the British parliamentary system, and the legal system is modelled on the <a href="/wiki/English_law#Common_law" title="English law">common law of England</a>. The head of state is <a href="/wiki/Charles_III" title="Charles III">King Charles III</a>, who holds the title <a href="/wiki/Monarchy_of_Belize" title="Monarchy of Belize">King of Belize</a>. The King lives in the United Kingdom, and is represented in Belize by the <a href="/wiki/Governor-General_of_Belize" title="Governor-General of Belize">governor-general</a>. Executive authority is exercised by the cabinet, which advises the governor-general and is led by the <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Belize" class="mw-redirect" title="Prime Minister of Belize">Prime Minister of Belize</a>, who is head of government. Cabinet ministers are members of the majority political party in parliament and usually hold elected seats within it concurrent with their cabinet positions.
</p><p>The bicameral <a href="/wiki/National_Assembly_of_Belize" class="mw-redirect" title="National Assembly of Belize">National Assembly of Belize</a> comprises a <a href="/wiki/House_of_Representatives_of_Belize" class="mw-redirect" title="House of Representatives of Belize">House of Representatives</a> and a <a href="/wiki/Senate_of_Belize" class="mw-redirect" title="Senate of Belize">Senate</a>. The 31 members of the House are popularly elected to a maximum five-year term and introduce legislation affecting the development of Belize. The governor-general appoints the 12 members of the Senate, with a Senate president selected by the members. The Senate is responsible for debating and approving bills passed by the House.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Legislative_power" class="mw-redirect" title="Legislative power">Legislative power</a> is vested in both the government and the <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_Belize" class="mw-redirect" title="Parliament of Belize">Parliament of Belize</a>. Constitutional safeguards include freedom of speech, press, worship, movement, and association. The judiciary is independent of the executive and the legislature.<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Members of the independent judiciary are appointed. The judicial system includes local magistrates grouped under the Magistrates' Court, which hears less serious cases. The <a href="/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Belize" title="Supreme Court of Belize">Supreme Court</a> (chief justice) hears murder and similarly serious cases, and the Court of Appeal hears appeals from convicted individuals seeking to have their sentences overturned. Defendants may, under certain circumstances, appeal their cases to the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Court_of_Justice" title="Caribbean Court of Justice">Caribbean Court of Justice</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Political_culture">Political culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=9" title="Edit section: Political culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 1935, elections were reinstated, but only 1.8 percent of the population was eligible to vote. In 1954, women were granted the right to vote.<sup id="cite_ref-clio_35-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-clio-35">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Since 1974, the party system in Belize has been dominated by the centre-left <a href="/wiki/People%27s_United_Party" title="People&#39;s United Party">People's United Party</a> and the centre-right <a href="/wiki/United_Democratic_Party_(Belize)" title="United Democratic Party (Belize)">United Democratic Party</a>, although other small parties took part in all levels of elections in the past. Though none of these small political parties has ever won any significant number of seats or offices, their challenge has been growing over the years.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_relations">Foreign relations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=10" title="Edit section: Foreign relations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Belize" title="Foreign relations of Belize">Foreign relations of Belize</a></div>
<p>Belize is a full participating member of the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a>; the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations" title="Commonwealth of Nations">Commonwealth of Nations</a>; the <a href="/wiki/Organization_of_American_States" title="Organization of American States">Organization of American States</a> (OAS); the <a href="/wiki/Central_American_Integration_System" title="Central American Integration System">Central American Integration System</a> (SICA); the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Community" title="Caribbean Community">Caribbean Community</a> (CARICOM); the <a href="/wiki/CARICOM_Single_Market_and_Economy" title="CARICOM Single Market and Economy">CARICOM Single Market and Economy</a> (CSME); the <a href="/wiki/Association_of_Caribbean_States" title="Association of Caribbean States">Association of Caribbean States</a> (ACS); and the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Court_of_Justice" title="Caribbean Court of Justice">Caribbean Court of Justice</a> (CCJ), which currently serves as a final court of appeal for only Barbados, Belize, and Guyana. In 2001 the Caribbean Community heads of government voted on a measure declaring that the region should work towards replacing the UK's <a href="/wiki/Judicial_Committee_of_the_Privy_Council" title="Judicial Committee of the Privy Council">Judicial Committee of the Privy Council</a> as final court of appeal with the Caribbean Court of Justice. It is still in the process of acceding to CARICOM treaties including the trade and single market treaties.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Royal_Marines_feel_the_heat_in_the_jungle_of_Belize_MOD_45162175.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/34/Royal_Marines_feel_the_heat_in_the_jungle_of_Belize_MOD_45162175.jpg/220px-Royal_Marines_feel_the_heat_in_the_jungle_of_Belize_MOD_45162175.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/34/Royal_Marines_feel_the_heat_in_the_jungle_of_Belize_MOD_45162175.jpg/330px-Royal_Marines_feel_the_heat_in_the_jungle_of_Belize_MOD_45162175.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/34/Royal_Marines_feel_the_heat_in_the_jungle_of_Belize_MOD_45162175.jpg/440px-Royal_Marines_feel_the_heat_in_the_jungle_of_Belize_MOD_45162175.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3600" data-file-height="2400" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Royal_Marines_feel_the_heat_in_the_jungle_of_Belize_MOD_45162175.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A British <a href="/wiki/Royal_Marine" class="mw-redirect" title="Royal Marine">Royal Marine</a> training in the jungle of Belize in 2017</div></div></div>
<p>Belize is an original member (1995) of the <a href="/wiki/World_Trade_Organization" title="World Trade Organization">World Trade Organization</a> (WTO), and participates actively in its work. The pact involves the Caribbean Forum (<a href="/wiki/CARIFORUM" title="CARIFORUM">CARIFORUM</a>) subgroup of the <a href="/wiki/African,_Caribbean_and_Pacific_Group_of_States" class="mw-redirect" title="African, Caribbean and Pacific Group of States">Group of African, Caribbean, and Pacific states</a> (ACP). CARIFORUM presently the only part of the wider ACP-bloc that has concluded the full regional trade-pact with the <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a>.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/British_Army_Training_and_Support_Unit_Belize" title="British Army Training and Support Unit Belize">British Army Garrison</a> in Belize is used primarily for <a href="/wiki/Jungle_warfare" title="Jungle warfare">jungle warfare</a> training, with access to over 13,000 square kilometres (5,000&#160;sq&#160;mi) of jungle terrain.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Belize is a party to the <a href="/wiki/Rome_Statute_of_the_International_Criminal_Court" class="mw-redirect" title="Rome Statute of the International Criminal Court">Rome Statute of the International Criminal Court</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Armed_forces">Armed forces</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Armed forces">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Military_of_Belize" class="mw-redirect" title="Military of Belize">Military of Belize</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Belize_Coast_Guard_and_U.S._Navy_Working_Together.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8c/Belize_Coast_Guard_and_U.S._Navy_Working_Together.jpg/220px-Belize_Coast_Guard_and_U.S._Navy_Working_Together.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8c/Belize_Coast_Guard_and_U.S._Navy_Working_Together.jpg/330px-Belize_Coast_Guard_and_U.S._Navy_Working_Together.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8c/Belize_Coast_Guard_and_U.S._Navy_Working_Together.jpg/440px-Belize_Coast_Guard_and_U.S._Navy_Working_Together.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4584" data-file-height="3051" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Belize_Coast_Guard_and_U.S._Navy_Working_Together.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Belizean Coast Guard working with the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Navy" title="United States Navy">United States Navy</a></div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Belize_Defence_Force" title="Belize Defence Force">Belize Defence Force</a> (BDF) serves as the country's military and is responsible for protecting the sovereignty of Belize. The BDF, with the Belize National Coast Guard and the Immigration Department, is a department of the Ministry of Defence and Immigration. In 1997 the regular army numbered over 900, the reserve army 381, the air wing 45 and the maritime wing 36, amounting to an overall strength of approximately 1,400.<sup id="cite_ref-cavehill.uwi.edu_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cavehill.uwi.edu-42">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup> In 2005, the maritime wing became part of the Belizean Coast Guard.<sup id="cite_ref-ra_20071213_43-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ra_20071213-43">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup> In 2012, the Belizean government spent about $17 million on the military, constituting 1.08% of the country's <a href="/wiki/Gross_domestic_product" title="Gross domestic product">gross domestic product</a> (GDP).<sup id="cite_ref-CIAFactbookMilitary2019_44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIAFactbookMilitary2019-44">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup>
After Belize achieved independence in 1981 the United Kingdom maintained a deterrent force (British Forces Belize) in the country to protect it from invasion by <a href="/wiki/Guatemala" title="Guatemala">Guatemala</a> (see <a href="/wiki/Guatemalan_claim_to_Belizean_territory" class="mw-redirect" title="Guatemalan claim to Belizean territory">Guatemalan claim to Belizean territory</a>). During the 1980s this included a battalion and <a href="/wiki/No._1417_Flight_RAF" title="No. 1417 Flight RAF">No. 1417 Flight RAF</a> of Harriers. The main British force left in 1994, three years after Guatemala recognized Belizean independence, but the United Kingdom maintained a training presence via the British Army Training and Support Unit Belize (BATSUB) and <a href="/wiki/25_Flight_AAC" class="mw-redirect" title="25 Flight AAC">25 Flight AAC</a> until 2011 when the last British Forces left Ladyville Barracks, with the exception of seconded advisers.<sup id="cite_ref-cavehill.uwi.edu_42-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-cavehill.uwi.edu-42">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_divisions">Administrative divisions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Administrative divisions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Belize,_administrative_divisions_-_en_-_monochrome.svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/Belize%2C_administrative_divisions_-_en_-_monochrome.svg/220px-Belize%2C_administrative_divisions_-_en_-_monochrome.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="305" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/Belize%2C_administrative_divisions_-_en_-_monochrome.svg/330px-Belize%2C_administrative_divisions_-_en_-_monochrome.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d7/Belize%2C_administrative_divisions_-_en_-_monochrome.svg/440px-Belize%2C_administrative_divisions_-_en_-_monochrome.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="788" data-file-height="1092" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Belize,_administrative_divisions_-_en_-_monochrome.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Districts of Belize</div></div></div>
<p>Belize is divided into <a href="/wiki/Districts_of_Belize" title="Districts of Belize">six districts</a>.
</p>
