<p>These districts are further divided into <a href="/wiki/Constituencies_of_Belize" title="Constituencies of Belize">31 constituencies</a>. <a href="/wiki/Local_government_in_Belize" title="Local government in Belize">Local government in Belize</a> comprises four types of local authorities: <a href="/wiki/City_council" class="mw-redirect" title="City council">city councils</a>, <a href="/wiki/Town_council" title="Town council">town councils</a>, <a href="/wiki/Town_council" title="Town council">village councils</a> and <a href="/wiki/Community_council" title="Community council">community councils</a>. The two city councils (<a href="/wiki/Belize_City" title="Belize City">Belize City</a> and <a href="/wiki/Belmopan" title="Belmopan">Belmopan</a>) and seven town councils cover the urban population of the country, while village and community councils cover the rural population.<sup id="cite_ref-belgov_46-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-belgov-46">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Guatemalan_territorial_dispute">Guatemalan territorial dispute</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Guatemalan territorial dispute">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Belizean%E2%80%93Guatemalan_territorial_dispute" title="Belizean–Guatemalan territorial dispute">Belizean–Guatemalan territorial dispute</a></div>
<p>Throughout Belize's history, Guatemala has <a href="/wiki/Belizean%E2%80%93Guatemalan_territorial_dispute" title="Belizean–Guatemalan territorial dispute">claimed sovereignty</a> over all or part of Belizean territory. This claim is occasionally reflected in maps drawn by Guatemala's government, showing Belize as Guatemala's <a href="/wiki/Departments_of_Guatemala" title="Departments of Guatemala">twenty-third department</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-CIAFactbookTransnational2019_47-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIAFactbookTransnational2019-47">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;b&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Guatemalan territorial claim involves approximately 53% of Belize's mainland, which includes significant portions of four districts: Belize, Cayo, Stann Creek, and Toledo.<sup id="cite_ref-50" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-50">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> Roughly 43% of the country's population (≈154,949 Belizeans) reside in this region.<sup id="cite_ref-51" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-51">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As of 2020<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, the border dispute with Guatemala remains unresolved and contentious.<sup id="cite_ref-CIAFactbookTransnational2019_47-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIAFactbookTransnational2019-47">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-53" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-53">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> Guatemala's claim to Belizean territory rests, in part, on Clause VII of the <a href="/wiki/Anglo-Guatemalan_Treaty_of_1859" class="mw-redirect" title="Anglo-Guatemalan Treaty of 1859">Anglo-Guatemalan Treaty of 1859</a>, which obligated the British to build a road between Belize City and Guatemala. At various times, the issue has required mediation by the United Kingdom, <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Community" title="Caribbean Community">Caribbean Community</a> heads of government, the <a href="/wiki/Organization_of_American_States" title="Organization of American States">Organization of American States</a> (OAS), Mexico, and the United States. On 15 April 2018, Guatemala's government held a referendum to determine if the country should take its <a href="/wiki/Belizean%E2%80%93Guatemalan_territorial_dispute" title="Belizean–Guatemalan territorial dispute">territorial claim on Belize</a> to the <a href="/wiki/International_Court_of_Justice" title="International Court of Justice">International Court of Justice</a> (ICJ) to settle the long-standing issue. Guatemalans voted 95%<sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> yes on the matter.<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> A similar referendum was to be held in Belize on 10 April 2019, but a court ruling led to its postponement.<sup id="cite_ref-56" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-56">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> The referendum was held on 8 May 2019, and 55.4% of voters opted to send the matter to the ICJ.<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Both countries submitted requests to the ICJ (in 2018 and 2019, respectively) and the ICJ ordered Guatemala's initial brief be submitted by December 2020 and Belize's response by 2022.<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Indigenous_land_claims">Indigenous land claims</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Indigenous land claims">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Belize backed the <a href="/wiki/Declaration_on_the_Rights_of_Indigenous_Peoples" title="Declaration on the Rights of Indigenous Peoples">United Nations (UN) Declaration on the Rights of Indigenous Peoples</a> in 2007, which established legal land rights to indigenous groups.<sup id="cite_ref-59" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-59">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> Other court cases have affirmed these rights like the <a href="/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Belize" title="Supreme Court of Belize">Supreme Court of Belize</a>'s 2013 decision to uphold its ruling in 2010 that acknowledges customary land titles as communal land for indigenous peoples.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> Another such case is the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Court_of_Justice" title="Caribbean Court of Justice">Caribbean Court of Justice</a>'s (CCJ) 2015 order on the Belizean government, which stipulated that the country develop a land registry to classify and exercise traditional governance over Mayan lands. Despite these rulings, Belize has made little progress to support the land rights of indigenous communities; for instance, in the two years since the CCJ's decision, Belize's government has failed to launch the Mayan land registry, prompting the group to take action into its own hands.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The exact ramifications of these cases need to be examined. As of 2017<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, Belize still struggles to recognize indigenous populations and their respective rights. According to the 50-page voluntary national report Belize created on its progress toward the <a href="/wiki/Sustainable_Development_Goals" title="Sustainable Development Goals">UN's 2030 Sustainable Development Goals</a>, indigenous groups are not factored into the country's indicators whatsoever.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> In fact, the groups 'Creole' and 'Garinagu' are not included in the document, and 'Maya' and 'Mestizo' only occur once throughout the entirety of the report.<sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> As of October 2018<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup> it is yet to be seen if the Belizean government will highlight the consequences of the territorial claim on indigenous land rights prior to the referendum vote in 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_Belize" title="Geography of Belize">Geography of Belize</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Belize_Topography.png" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e3/Belize_Topography.png/220px-Belize_Topography.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="309" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e3/Belize_Topography.png/330px-Belize_Topography.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e3/Belize_Topography.png/440px-Belize_Topography.png 2x" data-file-width="1419" data-file-height="1991" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Belize_Topography.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Topography of Belize</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Junior-Jaguar-Belize-Zoo.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/Junior-Jaguar-Belize-Zoo.jpg/220px-Junior-Jaguar-Belize-Zoo.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="266" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/Junior-Jaguar-Belize-Zoo.jpg/330px-Junior-Jaguar-Belize-Zoo.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/Junior-Jaguar-Belize-Zoo.jpg/440px-Junior-Jaguar-Belize-Zoo.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2130" data-file-height="2575" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Junior-Jaguar-Belize-Zoo.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Belizean jungles are home to the <a href="/wiki/Jaguar" title="Jaguar">jaguar</a> and many other mammals. <a href="/wiki/Cockscomb_Basin_Wildlife_Sanctuary" title="Cockscomb Basin Wildlife Sanctuary">Cockscomb Basin Wildlife Sanctuary</a> was founded in 1990 as the first <a href="/wiki/Wilderness" title="Wilderness">wilderness</a> sanctuary for the <a href="/wiki/Jaguar" title="Jaguar">jaguar</a> and is regarded by one author as the premier site for jaguar preservation in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated1996_66-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated1996-66">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup></div></div></div>
<p>Belize is on the Caribbean coast of northern Central America. It shares a border on the north with the Mexican state of <a href="/wiki/Quintana_Roo" title="Quintana Roo">Quintana Roo</a>, on the west with the Guatemalan department of <a href="/wiki/Pet%C3%A9n_(department)" class="mw-redirect" title="Petén (department)">Petén</a>, and on the south with the Guatemalan department of <a href="/wiki/Izabal_Department" title="Izabal Department">Izabal</a>. To the east in the Caribbean Sea, <a href="/wiki/Belize_Barrier_Reef" title="Belize Barrier Reef">the second-longest barrier reef</a> in the world flanks much of the 386 kilometres (240&#160;mi) of predominantly <a href="/wiki/Marsh" title="Marsh">marshy</a> coastline.<sup id="cite_ref-67" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-67">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> The area of the country totals 22,960 square kilometres (8,865&#160;sq&#160;mi), an area slightly larger than El Salvador, Israel, <a href="/wiki/New_Jersey" title="New Jersey">New Jersey</a>, or <a href="/wiki/Wales" title="Wales">Wales</a>. The many <a href="/wiki/Lagoon" title="Lagoon">lagoons</a> along the coasts and in the northern interior reduces the actual land area to 21,400 square kilometres (8,263&#160;sq&#160;mi). It is the only Central American country with no Pacific coastline.
</p><p>Belize is shaped roughly like a rhombus that extends about 280 kilometres (174&#160;mi) north-south and about 100 kilometres (62&#160;mi) east-west, with a total land boundary length of 516 kilometres (321&#160;mi). The undulating courses of two rivers, the <a href="/wiki/Rio_Hondo_(Belize)" class="mw-redirect" title="Rio Hondo (Belize)">Hondo</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Sarstoon_River" title="Sarstoon River">Sarstoon River</a>, define much of the course of the country's northern and southern boundaries. The western border follows no natural features and runs north–south through lowland forest and highland plateau.
</p><p>The north of Belize consists mostly of flat, swampy coastal plains, in places heavily forested. The <a href="/wiki/Flora_and_vegetation_of_Belize" class="mw-redirect" title="Flora and vegetation of Belize">flora</a> is highly diverse considering the small geographical area. The south contains the low <a href="/wiki/Mountain_range" title="Mountain range">mountain range</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Maya_Mountains" title="Maya Mountains">Maya Mountains</a>. The highest point in Belize is <a href="/wiki/Doyle%27s_Delight" title="Doyle&#39;s Delight">Doyle's Delight</a> at 1,124&#160;m (3,688&#160;ft).<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Belize's rugged geography has also made the country's coastline and jungle attractive to drug smugglers, who use the country as a gateway into Mexico.<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> In 2011, the United States added Belize to the list of nations considered major drug producers or transit countries for narcotics.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Environment_preservation_and_biodiversity">Environment preservation and biodiversity</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Environment preservation and biodiversity">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/List_of_protected_areas_of_Belize" title="List of protected areas of Belize">List of protected areas of Belize</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Scarlett_Macaw.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7e/Scarlett_Macaw.jpg/220px-Scarlett_Macaw.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7e/Scarlett_Macaw.jpg/330px-Scarlett_Macaw.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7e/Scarlett_Macaw.jpg/440px-Scarlett_Macaw.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1920" data-file-height="1280" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Scarlett_Macaw.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Scarlet_macaw" title="Scarlet macaw">Scarlet macaws</a> are native to Central and northern South America. Various <a href="/wiki/Bird_sanctuaries" class="mw-redirect" title="Bird sanctuaries">bird sanctuaries</a> exist in Belize, such as the <a href="/wiki/Crooked_Tree_Wildlife_Sanctuary" title="Crooked Tree Wildlife Sanctuary">Crooked Tree Wildlife Sanctuary</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>Belize has a rich variety of wildlife because of its position between <a href="/wiki/North_America" title="North America">North</a> and South America and a wide range of climates and habitats for plant and animal life.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> Belize's low human population and approximately 22,970 square kilometres (8,867&#160;sq&#160;mi) of undistributed land make for an ideal home for the more than 5,000 species of plants and hundreds of species of animals, including <a href="/wiki/Armadillo" title="Armadillo">armadillos</a>, snakes, and <a href="/wiki/Monkey" title="Monkey">monkeys</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Cockscomb_Basin_Wildlife_Sanctuary" title="Cockscomb Basin Wildlife Sanctuary">Cockscomb Basin Wildlife Sanctuary</a> is a nature reserve in south-central Belize established to protect the forests, fauna, and <a href="/wiki/Drainage_basin" title="Drainage basin">watersheds</a> of an approximately 400&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (150&#160;sq&#160;mi) area of the eastern slopes of the Maya Mountains. The reserve was founded in 1990 as the first wilderness sanctuary for the <a href="/wiki/Jaguar" title="Jaguar">jaguar</a> and is regarded by one author as the premier site for jaguar preservation in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated1996_66-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated1996-66">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Vegetation_and_flora">Vegetation and flora</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Vegetation and flora">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>While over 60% of Belize's land surface is covered by lush forest,<sup id="cite_ref-servir.net_74-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-servir.net-74">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> some 20% of the country's land is covered by cultivated land (agriculture) and human settlements.<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> Belize had a 2018 <a href="/wiki/Forest_Landscape_Integrity_Index" title="Forest Landscape Integrity Index">Forest Landscape Integrity Index</a> mean score of 6.15/10, ranking it 85th globally out of 172 countries.<sup id="cite_ref-FLII-Supplementary_76-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FLII-Supplementary-76">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Savanna" title="Savanna">Savanna</a>, scrubland and <a href="/wiki/Wetland" title="Wetland">wetland</a> constitute the remainder of Belize's land cover. Important <a href="/wiki/Mangrove" title="Mangrove">mangrove</a> ecosystems are also represented across Belize's landscape.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> Four terrestrial ecoregions lie within the country's borders – the <a href="/wiki/Pet%C3%A9n%E2%80%93Veracruz_moist_forests" title="Petén–Veracruz moist forests">Petén–Veracruz moist forests</a>, <a href="/wiki/Belizian_pine_forests" class="mw-redirect" title="Belizian pine forests">Belizian pine forests</a>, <a href="/wiki/Belizean_Coast_mangroves" title="Belizean Coast mangroves">Belizean Coast mangroves</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Belizean_Reef_mangroves" class="mw-redirect" title="Belizean Reef mangroves">Belizean Reef mangroves</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-DinersteinOlson2017_79-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DinersteinOlson2017-79">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> As a part of the globally significant <a href="/wiki/Mesoamerican_Biological_Corridor" title="Mesoamerican Biological Corridor">Mesoamerican Biological Corridor</a> that stretches from southern Mexico to Panama, Belize's biodiversity – both <a href="/wiki/Marine_(ocean)" class="mw-redirect" title="Marine (ocean)">marine</a> and <a href="/wiki/Terrestrial_ecoregion" class="mw-redirect" title="Terrestrial ecoregion">terrestrial</a> – is rich, with abundant <a href="/wiki/Flora" title="Flora">flora</a> and <a href="/wiki/Fauna" title="Fauna">fauna</a>.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Cockscomb_Basin_Wildlife_Sanctuary_07.JPG" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/01/Cockscomb_Basin_Wildlife_Sanctuary_07.JPG/220px-Cockscomb_Basin_Wildlife_Sanctuary_07.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/01/Cockscomb_Basin_Wildlife_Sanctuary_07.JPG/330px-Cockscomb_Basin_Wildlife_Sanctuary_07.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/01/Cockscomb_Basin_Wildlife_Sanctuary_07.JPG/440px-Cockscomb_Basin_Wildlife_Sanctuary_07.JPG 2x" data-file-width="5472" data-file-height="3648" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Cockscomb_Basin_Wildlife_Sanctuary_07.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Cockscomb_Basin_Wildlife_Sanctuary" title="Cockscomb Basin Wildlife Sanctuary">Cockscomb Basin Wildlife Sanctuary</a></div></div></div>
<p>Belize is also a leader in protecting biodiversity and natural resources. According to the <a href="/wiki/World_Database_on_Protected_Areas" title="World Database on Protected Areas">World Database on Protected Areas</a>, 37% of Belize's land territory falls under some form of official protection, giving Belize one of the most extensive systems of terrestrial protected areas in the Americas.<sup id="cite_ref-ProtectedPlanetBZ_80-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ProtectedPlanetBZ-80">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> By contrast, Costa Rica only has 27% of its land territory protected.<sup id="cite_ref-ProtectedPlanetCR_81-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ProtectedPlanetCR-81">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Around 13.6% of Belize's <a href="/wiki/Territorial_waters" title="Territorial waters">territorial waters</a>, which contain the <a href="/wiki/Belize_Barrier_Reef" title="Belize Barrier Reef">Belize Barrier Reef</a>, are also protected.<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> The Belize Barrier Reef is a <a href="/wiki/UNESCO" title="UNESCO">UNESCO</a>-recognized <a href="/wiki/World_Heritage_Site" title="World Heritage Site">World Heritage Site</a> and is the second-largest barrier reef in the world, behind Australia's <a href="/wiki/Great_Barrier_Reef" title="Great Barrier Reef">Great Barrier Reef</a>.
</p><p>A <a href="/wiki/Remote_sensing" title="Remote sensing">remote sensing</a> study conducted by the Water Center for the Humid Tropics of Latin America and the Caribbean (CATHALAC) and <a href="/wiki/NASA" title="NASA">NASA</a>, in collaboration with the Forest Department and the Land Information Centre (LIC) of the government of Belize's Ministry of Natural Resources and the Environment (MNRE), and published in August 2010 revealed that Belize's <a href="/wiki/Forest_cover" title="Forest cover">forest cover</a> in early 2010 was approximately 62.7%, down from 75.9% in late 1980.<sup id="cite_ref-servir.net_74-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-servir.net-74">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> A similar study by Belize Tropical Forest Studies and Conservation International revealed similar trends in terms of Belize's forest cover.<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> Both studies indicate that each year, 0.6% of Belize's forest cover is lost, translating to the clearing of an average of 10,050 hectares (24,835 acres) each year. The <a href="/wiki/USAID" class="mw-redirect" title="USAID">USAID</a>-supported <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.servirglobal.net/">SERVIR</a> study by CATHALAC, NASA, and the MNRE also showed that Belize's protected areas have been extremely effective in protecting the country's forests. While only some 6.4% of forests inside of legally declared protected areas were cleared between 1980 and 2010, over a quarter of forests outside of protected areas were lost between 1980 and 2010.
</p><p>As a country with a relatively high forest cover and a low <a href="/wiki/Deforestation" title="Deforestation">deforestation</a> rate, Belize has significant potential for participation in initiatives such as <a href="/wiki/REDD" class="mw-redirect" title="REDD">REDD</a>. Significantly, the SERVIR study on Belize's deforestation<sup id="cite_ref-servir.net_74-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-servir.net-74">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> was also recognized by the <a href="/wiki/Group_on_Earth_Observations" title="Group on Earth Observations">Group on Earth Observations</a> (GEO), of which Belize is a member nation.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Natural_resources_and_energy">Natural resources and energy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Natural resources and energy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Belize is known to have a number of economically important minerals, but none in quantities large enough to warrant mining. These minerals include <a href="/wiki/Dolomite_(mineral)" title="Dolomite (mineral)">dolomite</a>, <a href="/wiki/Barite" class="mw-redirect" title="Barite">barite</a> (source of <a href="/wiki/Barium" title="Barium">barium</a>), <a href="/wiki/Bauxite" title="Bauxite">bauxite</a> (source of aluminium), <a href="/wiki/Cassiterite" title="Cassiterite">cassiterite</a> (source of tin), and gold. In 1990 <a href="/wiki/Limestone" title="Limestone">limestone</a>, used in road construction, was the only mineral resource exploited for domestic or export use.
</p><p>In 2006, the cultivation of newly discovered <a href="/wiki/Crude_oil" class="mw-redirect" title="Crude oil">crude oil</a> in the town of <a href="/wiki/Spanish_Lookout" title="Spanish Lookout">Spanish Lookout</a> has presented new prospects and problems for this developing nation.<sup id="cite_ref-Maya_Homeland_85-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Maya_Homeland-85">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Access to <a href="/wiki/Biocapacity" title="Biocapacity">biocapacity</a> in Belize is much higher than world average. In 2016, Belize had 3.8 global hectares<sup id="cite_ref-footprintdata_86-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-footprintdata-86">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> of biocapacity per person within its territory, much more than the world average of 1.6 global hectares per person.<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> In 2016 Belize used 5.4 global hectares of biocapacity per person – their <a href="/wiki/Ecological_footprint" title="Ecological footprint">ecological footprint</a> of consumption. This means they use more biocapacity than Belize contains. As a result, Belize is running a biocapacity deficit.<sup id="cite_ref-footprintdata_86-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-footprintdata-86">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Belize_Barrier_Reef">Belize Barrier Reef</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Belize Barrier Reef">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Belize_Barrier_Reef" title="Belize Barrier Reef">Belize Barrier Reef</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Belize_Barrier_Reef_Aerial_Looking_North.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Belize_Barrier_Reef_Aerial_Looking_North.jpg/170px-Belize_Barrier_Reef_Aerial_Looking_North.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="227" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Belize_Barrier_Reef_Aerial_Looking_North.jpg/255px-Belize_Barrier_Reef_Aerial_Looking_North.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/53/Belize_Barrier_Reef_Aerial_Looking_North.jpg/340px-Belize_Barrier_Reef_Aerial_Looking_North.jpg 2x" data-file-width="480" data-file-height="640" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Belize_Barrier_Reef_Aerial_Looking_North.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Belize Barrier Reef; aerial view looking north</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Great_Blue_Hole.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/61/Great_Blue_Hole.jpg/220px-Great_Blue_Hole.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="171" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/61/Great_Blue_Hole.jpg/330px-Great_Blue_Hole.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/61/Great_Blue_Hole.jpg/440px-Great_Blue_Hole.jpg 2x" data-file-width="662" data-file-height="515" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Great_Blue_Hole.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The <a href="/wiki/Great_Blue_Hole" title="Great Blue Hole">Great Blue Hole</a>, a phenomenon of <a href="/wiki/Karst_topography" class="mw-redirect" title="Karst topography">karst topography</a></div></div></div>
<p>The Belize Barrier Reef is a series of <a href="/wiki/Coral_reef" title="Coral reef">coral reefs</a> straddling the coast of Belize, roughly 300 metres (980&#160;ft) offshore in the north and 40 kilometres (25&#160;mi) in the south within the country limits. The Belize Barrier Reef is a 300-kilometre-long (190&#160;mi) section of the 900-kilometre-long (560&#160;mi) <a href="/wiki/Mesoamerican_Barrier_Reef_System" title="Mesoamerican Barrier Reef System">Mesoamerican Barrier Reef System</a>, which is continuous from <a href="/wiki/Canc%C3%BAn" title="Cancún">Cancún</a> on the northeast tip of the <a href="/wiki/Yucat%C3%A1n_Peninsula" title="Yucatán Peninsula">Yucatán Peninsula</a> through the <a href="/wiki/Riviera_Maya" title="Riviera Maya">Riviera Maya</a> up to <a href="/wiki/Honduras" title="Honduras">Honduras</a> making it one of the largest coral reef systems in the world.
</p><p>It is the top tourist destination in Belize, popular for <a href="/wiki/Scuba_diving" title="Scuba diving">scuba diving</a> and <a href="/wiki/Snorkelling" class="mw-redirect" title="Snorkelling">snorkelling</a>, and attracting almost half of its 260,000 visitors. It is also vital to its fishing industry.<sup id="cite_ref-BBC_News_-_12_June_2006_88-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBC_News_-_12_June_2006-88">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> In 1842 <a href="/wiki/Charles_Darwin" title="Charles Darwin">Charles Darwin</a> described it as "the most remarkable reef in the <a href="/wiki/West_Indies" title="West Indies">West Indies</a>".
</p><p>The Belize Barrier Reef was declared a <a href="/wiki/World_Heritage_Site" title="World Heritage Site">World Heritage Site</a> by <a href="/wiki/UNESCO" title="UNESCO">UNESCO</a> in 1996 due to its vulnerability and the fact that it contains important natural habitats for in-situ conservation of biodiversity.<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Species">Species</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Species">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The Belize Barrier Reef is home to a large diversity of plants and animals, and is one of the most diverse <a href="/wiki/Ecosystem" title="Ecosystem">ecosystems</a> of the world:
</p>
<ul><li>70 hard coral species</li>
<li>36 <a href="/wiki/Alcyonacea" title="Alcyonacea">soft coral</a> species</li>
<li>500 species of fish</li>
<li>hundreds of <a href="/wiki/Invertebrate" title="Invertebrate">invertebrate</a> species</li></ul>
<p>With ~90% of the reef still yet to be researched, some estimate that only 10% of all species have been discovered.<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Conservation">Conservation</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Conservation">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Belize became the first country in the world to completely ban <a href="/wiki/Bottom_trawling" title="Bottom trawling">bottom trawling</a> in December 2010.<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> In December 2015, Belize banned offshore oil drilling within 1&#160;km (0.6&#160;mi) of the Barrier Reef and all of its seven World Heritage Sites.<sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Despite these protective measures, the reef remains under threat from <a href="/wiki/Marine_pollution" title="Marine pollution">oceanic pollution</a> as well as uncontrolled tourism, shipping, and fishing. Other threats include hurricanes, along with global warming and the resulting increase in ocean temperatures,<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> which causes <a href="/wiki/Coral_bleaching" title="Coral bleaching">coral bleaching</a>. It is claimed by scientists that over 40% of Belize's coral reef has been damaged since 1998.<sup id="cite_ref-BBC_News_-_12_June_2006_88-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBC_News_-_12_June_2006-88">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Climate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Hurricanes_in_Belize" title="Hurricanes in Belize">Hurricanes in Belize</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:332px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Koppen-Geiger_Map_BLZ_present.svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f6/Koppen-Geiger_Map_BLZ_present.svg/330px-Koppen-Geiger_Map_BLZ_present.svg.png" decoding="async" width="330" height="266" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f6/Koppen-Geiger_Map_BLZ_present.svg/495px-Koppen-Geiger_Map_BLZ_present.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f6/Koppen-Geiger_Map_BLZ_present.svg/660px-Koppen-Geiger_Map_BLZ_present.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1056" data-file-height="850" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Koppen-Geiger_Map_BLZ_present.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Köppen climate classification of Belize</div></div></div>
<p>Belize has a <a href="/wiki/Tropical_climate" title="Tropical climate">tropical climate</a> with pronounced <a href="/wiki/Wet_season" title="Wet season">wet</a> and <a href="/wiki/Dry_season" title="Dry season">dry seasons</a>, although there are significant variations in weather patterns by region. Temperatures vary according to elevation, proximity to the coast, and the moderating effects of the northeast trade winds off the Caribbean. Average temperatures in the coastal regions range from 24&#160;°C (75.2&#160;°F) in January to 27&#160;°C (80.6&#160;°F) in July. Temperatures are slightly higher inland, except for the southern highland plateaus, such as the <a href="/wiki/Mountain_Pine_Ridge" class="mw-redirect" title="Mountain Pine Ridge">Mountain Pine Ridge</a>, where it is noticeably cooler year round. Overall, the seasons are marked more by differences in humidity and rainfall than in temperature.
</p><p>Average rainfall varies considerably, from 1,350 millimetres (53&#160;in) in the north and west to over 4,500 millimetres (180&#160;in) in the extreme south. Seasonal differences in rainfall are greatest in the northern and central regions of the country where, between January and April or May, less than 100 millimetres (3.9&#160;in) of rainfall per month. The dry season is shorter in the south, normally only lasting from February to April. A shorter, less rainy period, known locally as the "little dry", usually occurs in late July or August, after the onset of the rainy season.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Tropical_cyclone" title="Tropical cyclone">Hurricanes</a> have played key—and devastating—roles in <a href="/wiki/History_of_Belize" title="History of Belize">Belizean history</a>. In 1931, an unnamed hurricane destroyed over two-thirds of the buildings in Belize City and killed more than 1,000 people. In 1955, <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Janet" title="Hurricane Janet">Hurricane Janet</a> levelled the northern town of <a href="/wiki/Corozal_Town" title="Corozal Town">Corozal</a>. Only six years later, <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Hattie" title="Hurricane Hattie">Hurricane Hattie</a> struck the central coastal area of the country, with winds in excess of 300&#160;km/h (185&#160;mph) and 4&#160;m (13&#160;ft) <a href="/wiki/Storm_tide" class="mw-redirect" title="Storm tide">storm tides</a>. The devastation of Belize City for the second time in thirty years prompted the relocation of the capital some 80 kilometres (50&#160;mi) inland to the <a href="/wiki/Planned_city" class="mw-redirect" title="Planned city">planned city</a> of <a href="/wiki/Belmopan" title="Belmopan">Belmopan</a>.
</p><p>In 1978, <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Greta-Olivia" class="mw-redirect" title="Hurricane Greta-Olivia">Hurricane Greta</a> caused more than US$25&#160;million in damage along the southern coast. In 2000, <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Keith" title="Hurricane Keith">Hurricane Keith</a>, the wettest tropical cyclone in the nation's record, stalled, and hit the nation as a Category 4 storm on 1 October, causing 19 deaths and at least $280 million in damage. Soon after, on 9 October 2001, <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Iris" title="Hurricane Iris">Hurricane Iris</a> made landfall at <a href="/wiki/Monkey_River_Town" title="Monkey River Town">Monkey River Town</a> as a 235&#160;km/h (145&#160;mph) Category 4 storm. The storm demolished most of the homes in the village, and destroyed the banana crop. In 2007, <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Dean" title="Hurricane Dean">Hurricane Dean</a> made landfall as a Category 5 storm only 40&#160;km (25&#160;mi) north of the Belize–Mexico border. Dean caused extensive damage in northern Belize.
</p><p>In 2010, Belize was directly affected by the Category 2 <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Richard" title="Hurricane Richard">Hurricane Richard</a>, which made landfall approximately 32 kilometres (20&#160;mi) south-southeast of Belize City at around 00:45 UTC on 25 October 2010.<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> The storm moved inland towards <a href="/wiki/Belmopan" title="Belmopan">Belmopan</a>, causing estimated damage of <a href="/wiki/Belize_dollar" title="Belize dollar">BZ$</a>33.8&#160;million ($17.4&#160;million 2010&#160;USD), primarily from damage to crops and housing.<sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The most recent hurricane to affect the nation was <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Lisa_(2022)" class="mw-redirect" title="Hurricane Lisa (2022)">Hurricane Lisa</a> in 2022.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Economy">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Belize" title="Economy of Belize">Economy of Belize</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:312px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Belize_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Belize_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/310px-Belize_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="310" height="202" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Belize_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/465px-Belize_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6d/Belize_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/620px-Belize_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Belize_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A proportional representation of Belize's exports in 2019</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Sugar_Cane_Processing_Plant_2.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/31/Sugar_Cane_Processing_Plant_2.jpg/220px-Sugar_Cane_Processing_Plant_2.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/31/Sugar_Cane_Processing_Plant_2.jpg/330px-Sugar_Cane_Processing_Plant_2.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/31/Sugar_Cane_Processing_Plant_2.jpg/440px-Sugar_Cane_Processing_Plant_2.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3872" data-file-height="2592" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Sugar_Cane_Processing_Plant_2.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A <a href="/wiki/Sugar_cane" class="mw-redirect" title="Sugar cane">sugar cane</a> processing plant, Orange Walk Town, Belize. Sugar is one of Belize's top exports.</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Belize_Caye_Caulker-221.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/35/Belize_Caye_Caulker-221.jpg/220px-Belize_Caye_Caulker-221.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="124" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/35/Belize_Caye_Caulker-221.jpg/330px-Belize_Caye_Caulker-221.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/35/Belize_Caye_Caulker-221.jpg/440px-Belize_Caye_Caulker-221.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3534" data-file-height="1985" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Belize_Caye_Caulker-221.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Panoramic view of <a href="/wiki/Caye_Caulker" title="Caye Caulker">Caye Caulker</a></div></div></div>
<p>Belize has a small, mostly private enterprise economy that is based primarily on agriculture, agro-based industry, and merchandising, with tourism and construction recently assuming greater importance.<sup id="cite_ref-Maya_Homeland_85-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Maya_Homeland-85">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> The country is also a producer of <a href="/wiki/Industrial_mineral" title="Industrial mineral">industrial minerals</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Crude_oil" class="mw-redirect" title="Crude oil">crude oil</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Petroleum" title="Petroleum">petroleum</a>. As of 2017<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, oil production was 320&#160;m<sup>3</sup>/d (2,000&#160;bbl/d).<sup id="cite_ref-EIA_98-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EIA-98">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> In agriculture, <a href="/wiki/Sugar" title="Sugar">sugar</a>, like in colonial times, remains the chief crop, accounting for nearly half of exports, while the <a href="/wiki/Banana" title="Banana">banana</a> industry is the largest employer.<sup id="cite_ref-Maya_Homeland_85-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Maya_Homeland-85">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> In 2007 Belize became the world's third largest exporter of <a href="/wiki/Papaya" title="Papaya">papaya</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The government of Belize faces important challenges to economic stability. Rapid action to improve <a href="/wiki/Tax_collection" class="mw-redirect" title="Tax collection">tax collection</a> has been promised, but a lack of progress in reining in spending could bring the <a href="/wiki/Exchange_rate" title="Exchange rate">exchange rate</a> under pressure. The tourist and construction sectors strengthened in early 1999, leading to a preliminary estimate of revived growth at four percent. Infrastructure remains a major economic development challenge;<sup id="cite_ref-USDoS-Belize_100-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-USDoS-Belize-100">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> Belize has the region's most expensive electricity. Trade is important and the major trading partners are the United States, Mexico, the United Kingdom, the <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Community" title="Caribbean Community">CARICOM</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-USDoS-Belize_100-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-USDoS-Belize-100">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Belize has four commercial bank groups, of which the largest and oldest is <a href="/wiki/Belize_Bank" title="Belize Bank">Belize Bank</a>. The other three banks are Heritage Bank, Atlantic Bank, and <a href="/wiki/Scotiabank" title="Scotiabank">Scotiabank</a> (Belize). A robust complex of <a href="/wiki/Credit_union" title="Credit union">credit unions</a> began in the 1940s under the leadership of <a href="/wiki/Marion_M._Ganey" title="Marion M. Ganey">Marion M. Ganey</a>, S.J.<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Because of its location on the coast of Central America, Belize is a popular destination for vacationers and for many North American drug traffickers. The Belize currency is pegged to the U.S. dollar and banks in Belize offer non-residents the ability to establish accounts, so drug traffickers and money launderers are attracted to banks in Belize. As a result, the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Department_of_State" title="United States Department of State">United States Department of State</a> has recently<sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Manual_of_Style/Dates_and_numbers#Chronological_items" title="Wikipedia:Manual of Style/Dates and numbers"><span title="The time period mentioned near this tag is ambiguous. (May 2022)">when?</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> named Belize one of the world's "major money laundering countries".<sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Industrial_infrastructure">Industrial infrastructure</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Industrial infrastructure">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:312px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Belize_electricity_production.svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/Belize_electricity_production.svg/310px-Belize_electricity_production.svg.png" decoding="async" width="310" height="221" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/Belize_electricity_production.svg/465px-Belize_electricity_production.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/8f/Belize_electricity_production.svg/620px-Belize_electricity_production.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="639" data-file-height="455" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Belize_electricity_production.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Belize electricity supply by source</div></div></div>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1097763485">.mw-parser-output .ambox{border:1px solid #a2a9b1;border-left:10px solid #36c;background-color:#fbfbfb;box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output .mbox-text-span{margin-left:23px!important}@media(min-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .ambox{margin:0 10%}}</style><table class="box-Update plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Update" class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This section needs to be <b>updated</b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help update this article to reflect recent events or newly available information.</span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">January 2014</span>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>The largest integrated electric utility and the principal distributor in Belize is <a href="/wiki/Belize_Electricity_Limited" title="Belize Electricity Limited">Belize Electricity Limited</a>. BEL was approximately 70% owned by <a href="/wiki/Fortis_Inc." title="Fortis Inc.">Fortis Inc.</a>, a Canadian investor-owned distribution utility. Fortis took over the management of BEL in 1999, at the invitation of the government of Belize in an attempt to mitigate prior financial problems with the locally managed utility. In addition to its regulated investment in BEL, Fortis owns Belize Electric Company Limited (BECOL), a non-regulated <a href="/wiki/Hydroelectric" class="mw-redirect" title="Hydroelectric">hydroelectric</a> generation business that operates three hydroelectric generating facilities on the <a href="/wiki/Macal_River" title="Macal River">Macal River</a>.
</p><p>On 14 June 2011, the government of Belize nationalized the ownership interest of Fortis Inc. in Belize Electricity Ltd. The utility encountered serious financial problems after the country's Public Utilities Commission (PUC) in 2008 "disallowed the recovery of previously incurred fuel and purchased power costs in customer rates and set customer rates at a level that does not allow BEL to earn a fair and reasonable return", Fortis said in a June 2011 statement.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> BEL appealed this judgement to the Court of Appeal, with a hearing expected in 2012. In May 2011, the <a href="/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Belize" title="Supreme Court of Belize">Supreme Court of Belize</a> granted BEL's application to prevent the PUC from taking any enforcement actions pending the appeal. The Belize Chamber of Commerce and Industry issued a statement saying the government had acted in haste and expressed concern over the message it sent to investors.
</p><p>In August 2009, the government of Belize nationalized <a href="/wiki/Belize_Telemedia_Limited" title="Belize Telemedia Limited">Belize Telemedia Limited</a> (BTL), which now competes directly with <a href="/wiki/Speednet" title="Speednet">Speednet</a>. As a result of the nationalization process, the <a href="/wiki/Interconnection" title="Interconnection">interconnection</a> agreements are again subject to negotiations. Both BTL and Speednet sell basic telephone services, national and international calls, prepaid services, cellular services via GSM 1900 megahertz (MHz) and 4G LTE respectively, international cellular roaming, fixed wireless, fibre-to-the-home internet service, and national and international data networks.<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Tourism">Tourism</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Tourism">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Tourism_in_Belize" title="Tourism in Belize">Tourism in Belize</a></div>
<p>A combination of natural factors – climate, the <a href="/wiki/Belize_Barrier_Reef" title="Belize Barrier Reef">Belize Barrier Reef</a>, over 450 offshore Cays (islands), excellent fishing, safe waters for boating, <a href="/wiki/Scuba_diving" title="Scuba diving">scuba diving</a>, <a href="/wiki/Snorkelling" class="mw-redirect" title="Snorkelling">snorkelling</a> and <a href="/wiki/Freediving" title="Freediving">freediving</a>, numerous rivers for <a href="/wiki/Rafting" title="Rafting">rafting</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Kayaking" title="Kayaking">kayaking</a>, various <a href="/wiki/Jungle_(terrain)" class="mw-redirect" title="Jungle (terrain)">jungle</a> and <a href="/wiki/Wildlife_reserves" class="mw-redirect" title="Wildlife reserves">wildlife reserves</a> of fauna and flora, for hiking, <a href="/wiki/Bird_watching" class="mw-redirect" title="Bird watching">bird watching</a>, and helicopter touring, as well as many Maya sites – support the thriving tourism and <a href="/wiki/Ecotourism" title="Ecotourism">ecotourism</a> industry.
</p><p>Development costs are high, but the government of Belize has made tourism its second development priority after agriculture. In 2012, tourist arrivals totalled 917,869 (with about 584,683 from the United States) and tourist receipts amounted to over $1.3 billion.<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>After <a href="/wiki/COVID-19" title="COVID-19">COVID-19</a> struck tourism, Belize became the first country in the Caribbean to allow vaccinated travelers to visit without a COVID-19 test.<sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Transport">Transport</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: Transport">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Transport_in_Belize" title="Transport in Belize">Transport in Belize</a></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Belize" title="Demographics of Belize">Demographics of Belize</a> and <a href="/wiki/Belizean_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Belizean people">Belizean people</a></div>
<p>Belize's population is estimated to be 441,471 in 2022.<sup id="cite_ref-est_7-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-est-7">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> Belize's <a href="/wiki/Total_fertility_rate" title="Total fertility rate">total fertility rate</a> in 2009 was 3.6 children per woman. Its birth rate was 22.9 births/1,000 population (2018 estimate), and the death rate was 4.2 deaths/1,000 population (2018 estimate).<sup id="cite_ref-CIAFactbookPeople2019_2-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIAFactbookPeople2019-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup> A substantial ethnic-demographic shift has been occurring since 1980 when the Creole/Mestizo ratio shifted from 58/38 to currently 26/53, due to many Creoles moving to the US and a rising Mestizo birth rate and migration from El Salvador.<sup id="cite_ref-107" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-107">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup>
</p>

<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="The_Maya">The Maya</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: The Maya">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The Maya are thought to have been in Belize and the <a href="/wiki/Yucat%C3%A1n_region" class="mw-redirect" title="Yucatán region">Yucatán region</a> since the second millennium BCE. Many died in conflicts between constantly warring tribes or by catching disease from invading Europeans. Three Maya groups now inhabit the country: The <a href="/wiki/Yucatec" class="mw-redirect" title="Yucatec">Yucatec</a> (who came from Yucatán, Mexico, to escape the savage <a href="/wiki/Caste_War_of_Yucat%C3%A1n" title="Caste War of Yucatán">Caste War</a> of the 1840s), the <a href="/wiki/Mopan_people" title="Mopan people">Mopan</a> (indigenous to Belize but were forced out to Guatemala by the British for raiding settlements; they returned to Belize to evade enslavement by the Guatemalans in the 19th century), and <a href="/wiki/Q%27eqchi%27_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Q&#39;eqchi&#39; people">Q'eqchi'</a> (also fled from slavery in Guatemala in the 19th century).<sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> The latter groups are chiefly found in the <a href="/wiki/Toledo_District" title="Toledo District">Toledo District</a>. The Maya speak their native languages and Spanish, and are also often fluent in English and Belize Kriol.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Creoles">Creoles</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Creoles">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Belizean_Creole_people" title="Belizean Creole people">Belizean Creole people</a> and <a href="/wiki/Baymen" title="Baymen">Baymen</a></div>
<p>Belizean Creoles are primarily <a href="/wiki/Mixed-race" class="mw-redirect" title="Mixed-race">mixed-raced</a> descendants of <a href="/wiki/African_slave_trade" class="mw-redirect" title="African slave trade">West and Central Africans</a> who were brought to the <a href="/wiki/British_Honduras" title="British Honduras">British Honduras</a> (present-day Belize along the <a href="/wiki/Bay_of_Honduras" class="mw-redirect" title="Bay of Honduras">Bay of Honduras</a>) as well as the <a href="/wiki/English_people" title="English people">English</a> and <a href="/wiki/Scottish_people" title="Scottish people">Scottish</a> log cutters, known as the <a href="/wiki/Baymen" title="Baymen">Baymen</a> who <a href="/wiki/Human_trafficking" title="Human trafficking">trafficked</a> them.<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-jstor.org_110-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-jstor.org-110">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup>  Over the years they have also intermarried with <a href="/wiki/Miskito_people" title="Miskito people">Miskito</a> from <a href="/wiki/Nicaragua" title="Nicaragua">Nicaragua</a>, <a href="/wiki/Jamaicans" title="Jamaicans">Jamaicans</a> and other <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_people" title="Caribbean people">Caribbean people</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hispanic_Belizean" class="mw-redirect" title="Hispanic Belizean">Mestizos</a>, <a href="/wiki/White_Caribbeans" title="White Caribbeans">Europeans</a>, <a href="/wiki/Garifuna" title="Garifuna">Garifunas</a>, <a href="/wiki/Maya_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Maya people">Mayas</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Ethnic_Chinese_in_Belize" title="Ethnic Chinese in Belize">Chinese</a> and <a href="/wiki/Asian_Indians_in_Belize" class="mw-redirect" title="Asian Indians in Belize">Indians</a>. The latter were brought to Belize as indentured laborers. Majority of Kriols trace their ancestry to several of the aforementioned groups.
For all intents and purposes, Creole is an ethnic and linguistic denomination. Some natives, even with blonde hair and blue eyes, may call themselves Creoles.<sup id="cite_ref-Johnson_2003_pp._598_111-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Johnson_2003_pp._598-111">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Belize_Creole_English" class="mw-redirect" title="Belize Creole English">Belize Creole English</a> or <i>Kriol</i> developed during the time of slavery, and historically was only spoken by former enslaved Africans. It became an integral part of the Belizean identity, and is now spoken by about 45% of Belizeans.<sup id="cite_ref-census2010_112-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-census2010-112">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Johnson_2003_pp._598_111-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Johnson_2003_pp._598-111">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> Belizean Creole is derived mainly from English. Its <a href="/wiki/Substratum" class="mw-redirect" title="Substratum">substrate languages</a> are the Native American language <a href="/wiki/Miskito_language" title="Miskito language">Miskito</a>, and the various <a href="/wiki/West_African" class="mw-redirect" title="West African">West African</a> and <a href="/wiki/Bantu_languages" title="Bantu languages">Bantu languages</a>, native languages of the enslaved Africans. Creoles are found all over Belize, but predominantly in urban areas such as Belize City, coastal towns and villages, and in the Belize River Valley.<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Garinagu">Garinagu</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Garinagu">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Garifuna_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Garifuna people">Garifuna people</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Garifuna_dancers_in_Dangriga,_Belize.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/Garifuna_dancers_in_Dangriga%2C_Belize.jpg/220px-Garifuna_dancers_in_Dangriga%2C_Belize.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="156" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/Garifuna_dancers_in_Dangriga%2C_Belize.jpg/330px-Garifuna_dancers_in_Dangriga%2C_Belize.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/Garifuna_dancers_in_Dangriga%2C_Belize.jpg/440px-Garifuna_dancers_in_Dangriga%2C_Belize.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1000" data-file-height="708" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Garifuna_dancers_in_Dangriga,_Belize.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Traditional Garifuna dancers in <a href="/wiki/Dangriga" title="Dangriga">Dangriga</a>, Belize</div></div></div>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Garinagu" class="mw-redirect" title="Garinagu">Garinagu</a> (singular <i>Garifuna</i>), at around 4.5% of the population, are a mix of West/Central African, <a href="/wiki/Arawaks" class="mw-redirect" title="Arawaks">Arawak</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Island_Carib" class="mw-redirect" title="Island Carib">Island Carib</a> ancestry. Though they were captives removed from their homelands, these people were never documented as slaves. The two prevailing theories are that, in 1635, they were either the survivors of two recorded shipwrecks or somehow took over the ship they came on.<sup id="cite_ref-www2.ku.edu_114-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-www2.ku.edu-114">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Throughout history they have been incorrectly labelled as <a href="/wiki/Black_Caribs" class="mw-redirect" title="Black Caribs">Black Caribs</a>. When the British took over <a href="/wiki/Saint_Vincent_and_the_Grenadines" title="Saint Vincent and the Grenadines">Saint Vincent and the Grenadines</a> after the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Paris_(1763)" title="Treaty of Paris (1763)">Treaty of Paris</a> in 1763, they were opposed by French settlers and their Garinagu allies. The Garinagu eventually surrendered to the British in 1796. The British separated the more African-looking Garifunas from the more indigenous-looking ones. 5,000 Garinagu were exiled from the Grenadine island of <a href="/wiki/Baliceaux" title="Baliceaux">Baliceaux</a>. About 2,500 of them survived the voyage to <a href="/wiki/Roat%C3%A1n" title="Roatán">Roatán</a>, an island off the coast of Honduras. The <a href="/wiki/Garifuna_language" title="Garifuna language">Garifuna language</a> belongs to the <a href="/wiki/Arawakan_languages" title="Arawakan languages">Arawakan language family</a>, but has a large number of loanwords from Carib languages and from English.
</p><p>Because <a href="/wiki/Roat%C3%A1n" title="Roatán">Roatán</a> was too small and infertile to support their population, the Garinagu petitioned the Spanish authorities of Honduras to be allowed to settle on the mainland coast. The Spanish employed them as soldiers, and they spread along the Caribbean coast of Central America. The Garinagu settled in Seine Bight, <a href="/wiki/Punta_Gorda,_Belize" title="Punta Gorda, Belize">Punta Gorda</a> and Punta Negra, Belize, by way of Honduras as early as 1802. In Belize, 19 November 1832 is the date officially recognized as "<a href="/wiki/Garifuna_Settlement_Day" title="Garifuna Settlement Day">Garifuna Settlement Day</a>" in Dangriga.<sup id="cite_ref-2000_Belize_Census_115-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2000_Belize_Census-115">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>According to one genetic study, their ancestry is on average 76% <a href="/wiki/Sub_Saharan_Africa" class="mw-redirect" title="Sub Saharan Africa">Sub Saharan African</a>, 20% <a href="/wiki/Arawak" title="Arawak">Arawak</a>/<a href="/wiki/Island_Carib" class="mw-redirect" title="Island Carib">Island Carib</a> and 4% <a href="/wiki/European_ethnic_groups" class="mw-redirect" title="European ethnic groups">European</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-www2.ku.edu_114-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-www2.ku.edu-114">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Mestizos">Mestizos</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Mestizos">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Mestizo" title="Mestizo">Mestizo</a></div>
<p>The Mestizo culture are people of mixed Spanish and Maya descent. They originally came to Belize in 1847, to escape the <a href="/wiki/Caste_War_of_Yucat%C3%A1n" title="Caste War of Yucatán">Caste War</a>, which occurred when thousands of Mayas rose against the state in Yucatán and massacred over one-third of the population. The surviving others fled across the borders into British territory. The Mestizos are found everywhere in Belize but most make their homes in the northern districts of Corozal and Orange Walk. Some other mestizos came from <a href="/wiki/El_Salvador" title="El Salvador">El Salvador</a>, <a href="/wiki/Guatemala" title="Guatemala">Guatemala</a>, <a href="/wiki/Honduras" title="Honduras">Honduras</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Nicaragua" title="Nicaragua">Nicaragua</a>. The Mestizos are the largest ethnic group in Belize and make up approximately half of the population. The Mestizo towns centre on a main square, and social life focuses on the Catholic Church built on one side of it. Spanish is the main language of most Mestizos and Spanish descendants, but many speak English and Belize Kriol fluently.<sup id="cite_ref-mybelizeadventure.com_116-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mybelizeadventure.com-116">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> Due to the influences of Kriol and English, many Mestizos speak what is known as <a href="/wiki/Spanglish" title="Spanglish">"Kitchen Spanish"</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-ambergriscaye.com_117-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ambergriscaye.com-117">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup> The mixture of Latin and Maya foods like tamales, escabeche, chirmole, relleno, and empanadas came from their Mexican side and corn tortillas were handed down by their Mayan side. Music comes mainly from the <a href="/wiki/Marimba" title="Marimba">marimba</a>, but they also play and sing with the guitar. Dances performed at village fiestas include the Hog-Head, Zapateados, the Mestizada, Paso Doble and many more.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="German-speaking_Mennonites">German-speaking Mennonites</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=33" title="Edit section: German-speaking Mennonites">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Mennonites_in_Belize" title="Mennonites in Belize">Mennonites in Belize</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Menonite_Children.JPG" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/7/76/Menonite_Children.JPG/220px-Menonite_Children.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/7/76/Menonite_Children.JPG/330px-Menonite_Children.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/7/76/Menonite_Children.JPG/440px-Menonite_Children.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2592" data-file-height="1944" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Menonite_Children.JPG" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Mennonites" title="Mennonites">Mennonite</a> children selling peanuts near Lamanai in Belize. Over 12,000 <a href="/wiki/Plautdietsch_language" title="Plautdietsch language">Plautdietsch</a>-speaking <a href="/wiki/Mennonites_in_Belize" title="Mennonites in Belize">Mennonites</a> live in Belize, farming the land and living according to their religious beliefs.<sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup></div></div></div>
<p>The majority of the <a href="/wiki/Mennonites" title="Mennonites">Mennonite</a> population comprises so-called <a href="/wiki/Russian_Mennonites" class="mw-redirect" title="Russian Mennonites">Russian Mennonites</a> of German descent who settled in the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Empire" title="Russian Empire">Russian Empire</a> during the 18th and 19th centuries. Most Russian Mennonites live in Mennonite settlements like <a href="/wiki/Spanish_Lookout" title="Spanish Lookout">Spanish Lookout</a>, <a href="/wiki/Shipyard,_Belize" title="Shipyard, Belize">Shipyard</a>, <a href="/wiki/Little_Belize" title="Little Belize">Little Belize</a>, and Blue Creek. These Mennonites speak <a href="/wiki/Plautdietsch" class="mw-redirect" title="Plautdietsch">Plautdietsch</a> (a <a href="/wiki/Low_German" title="Low German">Low German dialect</a>) in everyday life, but use mostly <a href="/wiki/Standard_German" title="Standard German">Standard German</a> for reading (the Bible) and writing. The Plautdietsch-speaking <a href="/wiki/Mennonites_in_Mexico" title="Mennonites in Mexico">Mennonites came mostly from Mexico</a> in the years after 1958 and they are trilingual with proficiency in Spanish. There are also some mainly <a href="/wiki/Pennsylvania_Dutch" title="Pennsylvania Dutch">Pennsylvania Dutch</a>-speaking <a href="/wiki/Old_Order_Mennonites" class="mw-redirect" title="Old Order Mennonites">Old Order Mennonites</a> who came from the United States and Canada in the late 1960s. They live primarily in <a href="/wiki/Upper_Barton_Creek" title="Upper Barton Creek">Upper Barton Creek</a> and associated settlements. These Mennonites attracted people from different <a href="/wiki/Anabaptist" class="mw-redirect" title="Anabaptist">Anabaptist</a> backgrounds who formed a new community. They look quite similar to <a href="/wiki/Amish" title="Amish">Old Order Amish</a>, but are different from them.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Other_groups">Other groups</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=34" title="Edit section: Other groups">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The remaining 5% or so of the population consist of a mix of <a href="/wiki/Indians_in_Belize" class="mw-redirect" title="Indians in Belize">Indians</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ethnic_Chinese_in_Belize" title="Ethnic Chinese in Belize">Chinese</a>, <a href="/wiki/White_people" title="White people">Whites</a> from the United Kingdom, United States and Canada, and many other foreign groups brought to assist the country's development. During the 1860s, a large influx of East Indians who spent brief periods in Jamaica and <a href="/wiki/American_Civil_War" title="American Civil War">American Civil War</a> veterans from <a href="/wiki/Louisiana" title="Louisiana">Louisiana</a> and other Southern states established <a href="/wiki/Confederate_settlements_in_British_Honduras" title="Confederate settlements in British Honduras">Confederate settlements in British Honduras</a> and introduced commercial sugar cane production to the colony, establishing 11 settlements in the interior. The 20th century saw the arrival of more Asian settlers from <a href="/wiki/Mainland_China" title="Mainland China">Mainland China</a>, India, <a href="/wiki/Syria" title="Syria">Syria</a> and <a href="/wiki/Lebanon" title="Lebanon">Lebanon</a>. <a href="/wiki/Said_Musa" title="Said Musa">Said Musa</a>, the son of an immigrant from <a href="/wiki/Palestine_(region)" title="Palestine (region)">Palestine</a>, was the <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Belize" class="mw-redirect" title="Prime Minister of Belize">Prime Minister of Belize</a> from 1998 to 2008. <a href="/wiki/Central_American" class="mw-redirect" title="Central American">Central American</a> immigrants from <a href="/wiki/El_Salvador" title="El Salvador">El Salvador</a>, <a href="/wiki/Guatemala" title="Guatemala">Guatemala</a>, <a href="/wiki/Honduras" title="Honduras">Honduras</a> and <a href="/wiki/Nicaragua" title="Nicaragua">Nicaragua</a> and expatriate Americans and Africans also began to settle in the country.<sup id="cite_ref-2000_Belize_Census_115-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2000_Belize_Census-115">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span id="Emigration.2C_immigration.2C_and_demographic_shifts"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Emigration,_immigration,_and_demographic_shifts">Emigration, immigration, and demographic shifts</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=35" title="Edit section: Emigration, immigration, and demographic shifts">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Creoles and other ethnic groups are emigrating mostly to the United States, but also to the United Kingdom and other developed nations for better opportunities. Based on the latest <a href="/wiki/US_Census" class="mw-redirect" title="US Census">US Census</a>, the number of Belizeans in the United States is approximately 160,000 (including 70,000 legal residents and naturalized citizens), consisting mainly of Creoles and Garinagu.<sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Because of conflicts in neighbouring Central American nations, Mestizo <a href="/wiki/Refugee" title="Refugee">refugees</a> from El Salvador, Guatemala, and Honduras have fled to Belize in significant numbers during the 1980s, and have been significantly adding to this group. These two events have been changing the demographics of the nation for the last 30 years.<sup id="cite_ref-paulglassman.com_120-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-paulglassman.com-120">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=36" title="Edit section: Languages">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
