<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_Belize" title="Languages of Belize">Languages of Belize</a></div>
<p>English is the official language of Belize. This stems from the country being a former British colony. Belize is the only country in Central America with English as the official language. Also, English is the primary language of public education, government and most media outlets. About half of Belizeans regardless of ethnicity speak a mostly <a href="/wiki/English-based_creole" class="mw-redirect" title="English-based creole">English-based creole</a> called <a href="/wiki/Belizean_Creole" title="Belizean Creole">Belize Creole</a> (or <i>Kriol</i> in Belize Creole). Although English is widely used, Kriol is spoken in all situations whether informal, formal, social or interethnic dialogue, even in meetings of the House of Representatives.
</p><p>When a Creole language exists alongside its <a href="/wiki/Lexifier" title="Lexifier">lexifier</a> language, as is the case in Belize, a continuum forms between the Creole and the lexifier language. It is therefore difficult to substantiate or differentiate the number of Belize Creole speakers compared to English speakers. Kriol might best be described as the <a href="/wiki/Lingua_franca" title="Lingua franca">lingua franca</a> of the nation.<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Approximately 50% of Belizeans self-identify as <i><a href="/wiki/Mestizo" title="Mestizo">Mestizo</a></i>, <i><a href="/wiki/Latino_sine_flexione" title="Latino sine flexione">Latino</a></i>, or <i>Hispanic</i> and 50–70% speak Caribbean Spanish as a native language.<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup> When Belize was a British colony, Spanish was banned in schools, but today it is widely spoken. <a href="/wiki/Spanglish" title="Spanglish">"Kitchen Spanish"</a> is an intermediate form of Spanish mixed with Belize Creole, spoken in the northern towns such as Corozal and San Pedro.<sup id="cite_ref-ambergriscaye.com_117-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ambergriscaye.com-117">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Over half the population is <a href="/wiki/Multilingual" class="mw-redirect" title="Multilingual">multilingual</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> Being a small, multiethnic state, surrounded by Spanish-speaking nations, the economic and social benefits from multilingualism are high.<sup id="cite_ref-belize2011_124-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-belize2011-124">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Belize is also home to three <a href="/wiki/Mayan_languages" title="Mayan languages">Maya languages</a>: <a href="/wiki/Q%27eqchi%27_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Q&#39;eqchi&#39; language">Q'eqchi'</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mopan_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Mopan language">Mopan</a> (an <a href="/wiki/Endangered_language" title="Endangered language">endangered language</a>), and <a href="/wiki/Yucatec_Maya_language" title="Yucatec Maya language">Yucatec Maya</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-126" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-126">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup>
Approximately 16,100 people speak the <a href="/wiki/Ta-Arawakan_languages" title="Ta-Arawakan languages">Arawakan-based</a> <a href="/wiki/Garifuna_language" title="Garifuna language">Garifuna language</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> and 6,900 Mennonites in Belize speak mainly <a href="/wiki/Plautdietsch_language" title="Plautdietsch language">Plautdietsch</a> while a minority of Mennonites speak <a href="/wiki/Pennsylvania_Dutch_language" title="Pennsylvania Dutch language">Pennsylvania Dutch</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Largest_cities">Largest cities</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=37" title="Edit section: Largest cities">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>

<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=38" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Belize" title="Religion in Belize">Religion in Belize</a></div>
<p>According to the 2010 census,<sup id="cite_ref-census2010_112-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-census2010-112">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> 40.1% of Belizeans are <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholics" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholics">Roman Catholics</a>, 31.8% are <a href="/wiki/Protestants" class="mw-redirect" title="Protestants">Protestants</a> (8.4% <a href="/wiki/Pentecostal" class="mw-redirect" title="Pentecostal">Pentecostal</a>; 5.4% <a href="/wiki/Adventist" class="mw-redirect" title="Adventist">Adventist</a>; 4.7% <a href="/wiki/Anglican" class="mw-redirect" title="Anglican">Anglican</a>; 3.7% <a href="/wiki/Mennonite" class="mw-redirect" title="Mennonite">Mennonite</a>; 3.6% <a href="/wiki/Baptist" class="mw-redirect" title="Baptist">Baptist</a>; 2.9% <a href="/wiki/Methodist" class="mw-redirect" title="Methodist">Methodist</a>; 2.8% <a href="/wiki/Church_of_the_Nazarene" title="Church of the Nazarene">Nazarene</a>), 1.7% are <a href="/wiki/Jehovah%27s_Witnesses" title="Jehovah&#39;s Witnesses">Jehovah's Witnesses</a>, 10.3% adhere to other religions (<a href="/wiki/Maya_religion" title="Maya religion">Maya religion</a>, <a href="/wiki/Garifuna_religion" class="mw-redirect" title="Garifuna religion">Garifuna religion</a>, <a href="/wiki/Obeah" title="Obeah">Obeah</a> and <a href="/wiki/Myalism" class="mw-redirect" title="Myalism">Myalism</a>, and minorities of <a href="/wiki/The_Church_of_Jesus_Christ_of_Latter-day_Saints" title="The Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints">the Church of Jesus Christ of Latter-day Saints</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hinduism" title="Hinduism">Hindus</a>, <a href="/wiki/Buddhism" title="Buddhism">Buddhists</a>, <a href="/wiki/Islam" title="Islam">Muslims</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_Faith" title="Baháʼí Faith">Baháʼís</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rastafarians" class="mw-redirect" title="Rastafarians">Rastafarians</a> and other) and 15.5% profess to be irreligious.
</p>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:HRrubenFromWest.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c3/HRrubenFromWest.jpg/220px-HRrubenFromWest.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="170" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c3/HRrubenFromWest.jpg/330px-HRrubenFromWest.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c3/HRrubenFromWest.jpg/440px-HRrubenFromWest.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3688" data-file-height="2856" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:HRrubenFromWest.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Holy_Redeemer_Catholic_Parish,_Belize_City" title="Holy Redeemer Catholic Parish, Belize City">Holy Redeemer Catholic Parish, Belize City</a></div></div></div>
<p>According to PROLADES, Belize was 64.6% Roman Catholic, 27.8% Protestant, 7.6% Other in 1971.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> Until the late 1990s, Belize was <a href="/wiki/History_of_Roman_Catholicism_in_Belize" class="mw-redirect" title="History of Roman Catholicism in Belize">a Roman Catholic majority country</a>. Catholics formed 57% of the population in 1991, and dropped to 49% in 2000. The percentage of Roman Catholics in the population has been decreasing in the past few decades due to the growth of Protestant churches, other religions and non-religious people.<sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In addition to Catholics, there has always been <a href="/wiki/Protestantism_in_Belize" class="mw-redirect" title="Protestantism in Belize">a large accompanying Protestant minority</a>. It was brought by <a href="/wiki/British_people" title="British people">British</a>, <a href="/wiki/German_people" class="mw-redirect" title="German people">German</a>, and other settlers to the British colony of <a href="/wiki/British_Honduras" title="British Honduras">British Honduras</a>. From the beginning, it was largely <a href="/wiki/Anglican" class="mw-redirect" title="Anglican">Anglican</a> and <a href="/wiki/Mennonite" class="mw-redirect" title="Mennonite">Mennonite</a> in nature. The Protestant community in Belize experienced a large <a href="/wiki/Pentecostal" class="mw-redirect" title="Pentecostal">Pentecostal</a> and <a href="/wiki/Seventh-Day_Adventist" class="mw-redirect" title="Seventh-Day Adventist">Seventh-Day Adventist</a> influx tied to the recent spread of various <a href="/wiki/Evangelical_Protestant" class="mw-redirect" title="Evangelical Protestant">Evangelical Protestant</a> denominations throughout <a href="/wiki/Latin_America" title="Latin America">Latin America</a>. Geographically speaking, <a href="/wiki/Mennonites_in_Belize" title="Mennonites in Belize">German Mennonites</a> live mostly in the rural districts of Cayo and Orange Walk.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Greek_Orthodox_Church" title="Greek Orthodox Church">Greek Orthodox Church</a> has a presence in <a href="/wiki/Santa_Elena,_Belize" class="mw-redirect" title="Santa Elena, Belize">Santa Elena</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Association_of_Religion_Data_Archives" title="Association of Religion Data Archives">Association of Religion Data Archives</a> estimates there were 7,776 Baháʼís in Belize in 2005, or 2.5% of the national population. Their estimates suggest this is the highest proportion of Baháʼís in any country.<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup> Their data also states that the Baháʼí Faith is the second most common religion in Belize, following Christianity.<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> Hinduism is followed by most Indian immigrants. <a href="/wiki/Sikhs" title="Sikhs">Sikhs</a> were the first <a href="/wiki/Indians_in_Belize" class="mw-redirect" title="Indians in Belize">Indian immigrants to Belize (not counting indentured workers)</a>, and the former <a href="/wiki/Chief_Justice_of_Belize" title="Chief Justice of Belize">Chief Justice of Belize</a> <a href="/wiki/George_Singh" title="George Singh">George Singh</a> was the son of a <a href="/wiki/Sikh_diaspora" title="Sikh diaspora">Sikh immigrant</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-News519980202_135-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-News519980202-135">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-SGV_136-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SGV-136">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> there was also a Sikh cabinet minister. Muslims claim that there have been Muslims in Belize since the 16th century having been brought over from Africa as slaves, but there are no sources for that claim.<sup id="cite_ref-official_137-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-official-137">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> The Muslim population of today started in the 1980s.<sup id="cite_ref-138" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-138">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup> Muslims numbered 243 in 2000 and 577 in 2010 according to the official statistics.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> and comprise 0.16 percent of the population. A mosque is at the Islamic Mission of Belize (IMB), also known as the Muslim Community of Belize. Another mosque, Masjid Al-Falah, officially opened in 2008 in Belize City.<sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Health">Health</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=39" title="Edit section: Health">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Healthcare_in_Belize" title="Healthcare in Belize">Healthcare in Belize</a></div>
<p>Belize has a high prevalence of communicable diseases such as respiratory diseases and intestinal illnesses.<sup id="cite_ref-MoH_141-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-MoH-141">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=40" title="Edit section: Education">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Education_in_Belize" title="Education in Belize">Education in Belize</a></div>
<p>A number of kindergartens, secondary, and tertiary schools in Belize provide quality education for students—mostly funded by the government. Belize has about a dozen <a href="/wiki/List_of_universities_in_Belize" title="List of universities in Belize">tertiary level institutions</a>, the most prominent of which is the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Belize" title="University of Belize">University of Belize</a>, which evolved out of the University College of Belize founded in 1986. Before that <a href="/wiki/St._John%27s_College,_Belize" title="St. John&#39;s College, Belize">St. John's College</a>, founded in 1877, dominated the tertiary education field. The Open Campus of the <a href="/wiki/University_of_the_West_Indies" title="University of the West Indies">University of the West Indies</a> has a site in Belize.<sup id="cite_ref-142" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-142">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> It also has campuses in <a href="/wiki/Barbados" title="Barbados">Barbados</a>, <a href="/wiki/Trinidad" title="Trinidad">Trinidad</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Jamaica" title="Jamaica">Jamaica</a>. The government of Belize contributes financially to the UWI.
</p><p>Education in Belize is compulsory between the ages of 6 and 14 years. As of 2010<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, the literacy rate in Belize was estimated at 79.7%,<sup id="cite_ref-census2010_112-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-census2010-112">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup> one of the <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_literacy_rate" title="List of countries by literacy rate">lowest in the Western Hemisphere</a>.
</p><p>The educational policy is currently following the "Education Sector Strategy 2011–2016", which sets three objectives for the years to come: Improving access, quality, and governance of the education system by providing technical and <a href="/wiki/Vocational_education" title="Vocational education">vocational education</a> and training.<sup id="cite_ref-143" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-143">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Crime">Crime</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=41" title="Edit section: Crime">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>Belize has moderate rates of violent crime.<sup id="cite_ref-state1_144-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-state1-144">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup> The majority of violence in Belize stems from gang activity, which includes trafficking of drugs and persons, protecting drug smuggling routes, and securing territory for drug dealing.<sup id="cite_ref-police2006_145-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-police2006-145">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2019, 102 murders were recorded in Belize, giving the country a homicide rate of 24 murders per 100,000 inhabitants, lower than the neighbouring countries of Mexico and <a href="/wiki/Honduras" title="Honduras">Honduras</a>, but higher than <a href="/wiki/Guatemala" title="Guatemala">Guatemala</a> and <a href="/wiki/El_Salvador" title="El Salvador">El Salvador</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-InSight_146-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-InSight-146">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Belize_District" title="Belize District">Belize District</a> (containing Belize City) had the most murders by far compared to all the other districts. In 2019, 58% of the murders occurred in the Belize District.<sup id="cite_ref-osac2019_147-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-osac2019-147">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup> The violence in Belize City (especially the southern part of the city) is largely due to gang warfare.<sup id="cite_ref-state1_144-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-state1-144">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2015, there were 40 reported cases of rape, 214 robberies, 742 burglaries, and 1027 cases of theft.<sup id="cite_ref-crime2015_148-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-crime2015-148">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Belize Police Department has implemented many protective measures in hopes of decreasing the high number of crimes. These measures include adding more patrols to "hot spots" in the city, obtaining more resources to deal with the predicament, creating the "Do the Right Thing for Youths at Risk" program, creating the Crime Information Hotline, creating the Yabra Citizen Development Committee, an organization that helps youth, and many other initiatives.<sup id="cite_ref-police2006_145-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-police2006-145">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup> In 2011, the government established a truce among many major gangs, lowering the murder rate.<sup id="cite_ref-state1_144-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-state1-144">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Social_structure">Social structure</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=42" title="Edit section: Social structure">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Belizean_society" title="Belizean society">Belizean society</a></div>
<p>Belize's social structure is marked by enduring differences in the distribution of wealth, power, and prestige. Because of the small size of Belize's population and the intimate scale of social relations, the social distance between the rich and the poor, while significant, is nowhere as vast as in other <a href="/wiki/Caribbean" title="Caribbean">Caribbean</a> and <a href="/wiki/Central_America" title="Central America">Central American</a> societies, such as <a href="/wiki/Jamaica" title="Jamaica">Jamaica</a> and <a href="/wiki/El_Salvador" title="El Salvador">El Salvador</a>. Belize lacks the violent class and racial conflict that has figured so prominently in the social life of its Central American neighbours.<sup id="cite_ref-sbs_149-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-sbs-149">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Political and economic power remain vested in the hands of the local elite. The sizeable middle group is composed of peoples of different ethnic backgrounds. This middle group does not constitute a unified <a href="/wiki/Social_class" title="Social class">social class</a>, but rather a number of middle-class and <a href="/wiki/Working-class" class="mw-redirect" title="Working-class">working-class</a> groups, loosely oriented around shared dispositions toward education, cultural respectability, and possibilities for upward social mobility. These beliefs, and the social practices they engender, help distinguish the middle group from the grass roots majority of the <a href="/wiki/Belizean_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Belizean people">Belizean people</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-sbs_149-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-sbs-149">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Women">Women</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=43" title="Edit section: Women">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>In 2021, the <a href="/wiki/World_Economic_Forum" title="World Economic Forum">World Economic Forum</a> ranked Belize 90th out of 156 countries in its <a href="/wiki/Global_Gender_Gap_Report" title="Global Gender Gap Report">Global Gender Gap Report</a>. Of all the countries in Latin America and the Caribbean, Belize ranked fourth from last. It ranked higher in the categories of "economic participation and opportunity" and "health and survival", but very low in "political empowerment".<sup id="cite_ref-ggr_150-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ggr-150">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup> In 2019, the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">UN</a> gave Belize a <a href="/wiki/Gender_Inequality_Index" title="Gender Inequality Index">Gender Inequality Index</a> score of 0.415, ranking it 97th out of 162 countries.<sup id="cite_ref-hdr_151-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hdr-151">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As of 2019<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, 49.9% of women in Belize participate in the workforce, compared to 80.6% of men.<sup id="cite_ref-hdr_151-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hdr-151">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup> 11.1% of the seats in Belize's <a href="/wiki/National_Assembly_(Belize)" title="National Assembly (Belize)">National Assembly</a> are filled by women.<sup id="cite_ref-hdr_151-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-hdr-151">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=44" title="Edit section: Culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Belize" title="Culture of Belize">Culture of Belize</a></div>
<p>In Belizean folklore, there are the legends of <a href="/wiki/Lang_Bobi_Suzi" title="Lang Bobi Suzi">Lang Bobi Suzi</a>, <a href="/wiki/La_Llorona" title="La Llorona">La Llorona</a>, <a href="/wiki/La_Sucia" class="mw-redirect" title="La Sucia">La Sucia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tata_Duende" title="Tata Duende">Tata Duende</a>, <a href="/wiki/Anansi" title="Anansi">Anansi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Xtabay" title="Xtabay">Xtabay</a>, Sisimite and the <a href="/wiki/Cadejo" title="Cadejo">cadejo</a>.
</p><p>Most of the <a href="/wiki/Public_holidays_in_Belize" title="Public holidays in Belize">public holidays in Belize</a> are traditional Commonwealth and <a href="/wiki/Liturgical_year" title="Liturgical year">Christian holidays</a>, although some are specific to Belizean culture such as <a href="/wiki/Garifuna_Settlement_Day" title="Garifuna Settlement Day">Garifuna Settlement Day</a> and Heroes and Benefactors' Day, formerly <a href="/wiki/Baron_Bliss_Day" class="mw-redirect" title="Baron Bliss Day">Baron Bliss Day</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-152" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-152">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup> In addition, the month of September is considered a <a href="/wiki/September_Celebrations_of_Belize" class="mw-redirect" title="September Celebrations of Belize">special time of national celebration</a> called September Celebrations with a whole month of activities on a special events calendar. Besides <a href="/wiki/Independence_Day" class="mw-redirect" title="Independence Day">Independence Day</a> and St. George's Caye Day, Belizeans also celebrate <a href="/wiki/Carnival" title="Carnival">Carnival</a> during September, which typically includes several events spread across multiple days, with the main event being the Carnival Road March, usually held the Saturday before 10 September. In some areas of Belize, it is celebrated at the traditional time before <a href="/wiki/Lent" title="Lent">Lent</a> (in February).<sup id="cite_ref-153" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-153">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cuisine">Cuisine</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=45" title="Edit section: Cuisine">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Cuisine_of_Belize" class="mw-redirect" title="Cuisine of Belize">Cuisine of Belize</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1097763485"/><table class="box-More_citations_needed plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Refimprove" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><a href="/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/50px-Question_book-new.svg.png" decoding="async" width="50" height="39" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/75px-Question_book-new.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/100px-Question_book-new.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="399" /></a></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This article <b>needs additional citations for <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">verification</a></b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help <a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit">improve this article</a> by <a href="/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners" title="Help:Referencing for beginners">adding citations to reliable sources</a>. Unsourced material may be challenged and removed.<br /><small><span class="plainlinks"><i>Find sources:</i>&#160;<a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="//www.google.com/search?as_eq=wikipedia&amp;q=%22Belize%22">"Belize"</a>&#160;–&#160;<a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="//www.google.com/search?tbm=nws&amp;q=%22Belize%22+-wikipedia&amp;tbs=ar:1">news</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="//www.google.com/search?&amp;q=%22Belize%22&amp;tbs=bkt:s&amp;tbm=bks">newspapers</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="//www.google.com/search?tbs=bks:1&amp;q=%22Belize%22+-wikipedia">books</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="//scholar.google.com/scholar?q=%22Belize%22">scholar</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.jstor.org/action/doBasicSearch?Query=%22Belize%22&amp;acc=on&amp;wc=on">JSTOR</a></span></small></span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">November 2020</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this template message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Rice_and_Beans,_Stew_Chicken_and_Potato_Salad_-_Belize.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7c/Rice_and_Beans%2C_Stew_Chicken_and_Potato_Salad_-_Belize.jpg/220px-Rice_and_Beans%2C_Stew_Chicken_and_Potato_Salad_-_Belize.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7c/Rice_and_Beans%2C_Stew_Chicken_and_Potato_Salad_-_Belize.jpg/330px-Rice_and_Beans%2C_Stew_Chicken_and_Potato_Salad_-_Belize.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7c/Rice_and_Beans%2C_Stew_Chicken_and_Potato_Salad_-_Belize.jpg/440px-Rice_and_Beans%2C_Stew_Chicken_and_Potato_Salad_-_Belize.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1824" data-file-height="1368" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Rice_and_Beans,_Stew_Chicken_and_Potato_Salad_-_Belize.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Rice and beans (with coconut milk), stewed chicken and potato salad. An inter-ethnic staple meal.</div></div></div>
<p>Belizean cuisine is an amalgamation of all ethnicities in the nation, and their respectively wide variety of foods. It might best be described as both similar to Mexican/Central American cuisine and Jamaican/Anglo-Caribbean cuisine but very different from these areas as well, with Belizean touches and innovation which have been handed down by generations. All immigrant communities add to the diversity of Belizean food, including the Indian and Chinese communities.
</p><p>The Belizean diet can be both very modern and traditional. There are no rules. Breakfast typically consists of bread, flour <a href="/wiki/Tortilla" title="Tortilla">tortillas</a>, or <a href="/wiki/Fry_jack" title="Fry jack">fry jacks</a> (deep fried dough pieces) that are often homemade. Fry jacks are eaten with various cheeses, "fry" beans, various forms of eggs or cereal, along with powdered milk, coffee, or tea. Tacos made from corn or flour tortillas and meat pies can also be consumed for a hearty breakfast from a street vendor. Midday meals are the main meals for Belizeans, usually called "dinner". They vary, from foods such as rice and beans with or without coconut milk, <a href="/wiki/Tamale" title="Tamale">tamales</a>, <a href="/wiki/Empanadas" class="mw-redirect" title="Empanadas">"panades"</a> (fried maize shells with beans or fish), meat pies, <a href="/wiki/Escabeche" title="Escabeche">escabeche</a> (onion soup), <a href="/wiki/Chimole" title="Chimole">chimole</a> (soup), <a href="/wiki/Caldo_de_pollo" title="Caldo de pollo">caldo</a>, stewed chicken, and garnaches (fried tortillas with beans, cheese, and sauce) to various constituted dinners featuring some type of rice and beans, meat and salad, or coleslaw. Fried "fry" chicken is another common course.
</p><p>In rural areas, meals are typically simpler than in cities. The Maya use <a href="/wiki/Maize" title="Maize">maize</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bean" title="Bean">beans</a>, or <a href="/wiki/Cucurbita" title="Cucurbita">squash</a> for most meals, and the Garifuna are fond of seafood, <a href="/wiki/Cassava" title="Cassava">cassava</a> (particularly made into cassava bread or ereba), and vegetables. The nation abounds with restaurants and fast food establishments that are fairly affordable. Local fruits are quite common, but raw vegetables from the markets less so. Mealtime is a communion for families and schools and some businesses close at midday for lunch, reopening later in the afternoon.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Music">Music</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=46" title="Edit section: Music">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Music_of_Belize" title="Music of Belize">Music of Belize</a></div>
<p>Bachata is the most popular genre of music along with <a href="/wiki/Punta" title="Punta">Punta</a> and has become one of the most popular kinds of music in Belize. It is distinctly Caribbean, and is sometimes said to be ready for international popularization like similarly descended styles (<a href="/wiki/Reggae" title="Reggae">reggae</a>, <a href="/wiki/Calypso_music" title="Calypso music">calypso</a>, <a href="/wiki/Merengue_(music)" class="mw-redirect" title="Merengue (music)">merengue</a>).
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Brukdown" title="Brukdown">Brukdown</a> is a modern style of Belizean music related to <a href="/wiki/Calypso_music" title="Calypso music">calypso</a>. It evolved out of the music and dance of loggers, especially a form called <a href="/wiki/Buru" title="Buru">buru</a>. <a href="/wiki/Reggae" title="Reggae">Reggae</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dance_hall" title="Dance hall">dance hall</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Soca_music" title="Soca music">soca</a> imported from <a href="/wiki/Jamaica" title="Jamaica">Jamaica</a> and the rest of the <a href="/wiki/West_Indies" title="West Indies">West Indies</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rapping" title="Rapping">rap</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hip-hop" class="mw-redirect" title="Hip-hop">hip-hop</a>, heavy metal and <a href="/wiki/Rock_music" title="Rock music">rock music</a> from the United States, are also popular among the youth of Belize.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sports">Sports</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=47" title="Edit section: Sports">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Sport_in_Belize" title="Sport in Belize">Sport in Belize</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Shalini_Zabaneh,_Cyclist,_Belize.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f3/Shalini_Zabaneh%2C_Cyclist%2C_Belize.jpg/220px-Shalini_Zabaneh%2C_Cyclist%2C_Belize.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f3/Shalini_Zabaneh%2C_Cyclist%2C_Belize.jpg/330px-Shalini_Zabaneh%2C_Cyclist%2C_Belize.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f3/Shalini_Zabaneh%2C_Cyclist%2C_Belize.jpg/440px-Shalini_Zabaneh%2C_Cyclist%2C_Belize.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="533" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Shalini_Zabaneh,_Cyclist,_Belize.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Accomplished Belizean cyclist Shalini Zabaneh</div></div></div>
<p>The major sports in Belize are <a href="/wiki/Association_football" title="Association football">football</a>, <a href="/wiki/Basketball" title="Basketball">basketball</a>, <a href="/wiki/Volleyball" title="Volleyball">volleyball</a> and <a href="/wiki/Cycle_sport" title="Cycle sport">cycling</a>, with smaller followings of <a href="/wiki/Boat_racing" title="Boat racing">boat racing</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sport_of_athletics" title="Sport of athletics">athletics</a>, <a href="/wiki/Softball" title="Softball">softball</a>, <a href="/wiki/Cricket" title="Cricket">cricket</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rugby_football" title="Rugby football">rugby</a> and <a href="/wiki/Netball" title="Netball">netball</a>. <a href="/wiki/Fishing" title="Fishing">Fishing</a> is also popular in coastal areas of Belize.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Cross_Country_Cycling_Classic" title="Cross Country Cycling Classic">Cross Country Cycling Classic</a>, also known as the "cross country" race or the Holy Saturday Cross Country Cycling Classic, is considered one of the most important Belize sports events. This one-day sports event is meant for amateur cyclists but has also gained worldwide popularity. The history of Cross Country Cycling Classic in Belize dates back to the period when <a href="/wiki/Monrad_Metzgen" title="Monrad Metzgen">Monrad Metzgen</a> picked up the idea from a small village on the <a href="/wiki/Northern_Highway_(Belize)" class="mw-redirect" title="Northern Highway (Belize)">Northern Highway</a> (now Phillip Goldson Highway). The people from this village used to cover long distances on their bicycles to attend the weekly game of cricket. He improvised on this observation by creating a sporting event on the difficult terrain of the <a href="/wiki/George_Price_Highway" title="George Price Highway">Western Highway</a>, which was then poorly built.
</p><p>Another major annual sporting event in Belize is the <a href="/wiki/La_Ruta_Maya_Belize_River_Challenge" class="mw-redirect" title="La Ruta Maya Belize River Challenge">La Ruta Maya Belize River Challenge</a>, a 4-day <a href="/wiki/Canoe_marathon" title="Canoe marathon">canoe marathon</a> held each year in March. The race runs from <a href="/wiki/San_Ignacio,_Belize" title="San Ignacio, Belize">San Ignacio</a> to <a href="/wiki/Belize_City" title="Belize City">Belize City</a>, a distance of 290 kilometres (180&#160;mi).<sup id="cite_ref-Peddicord_154-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Peddicord-154">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On Easter day, citizens of Dangriga participate in a yearly fishing tournament. First, second, and third prize are awarded based on a scoring combination of size, species, and number. The tournament is broadcast over local radio stations, and prize money is awarded to the winners.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Belize_national_basketball_team" class="mw-redirect" title="Belize national basketball team">Belize national basketball team</a> is the only national team that has achieved major victories internationally. The team won the 1998 CARICOM Men's Basketball Championship, held at the Civic Centre in Belize City, and subsequently participated in the 1999 Centrobasquet Tournament in Havana. The national team finished seventh of eight teams after winning only 1 game despite playing close all the way. In a return engagement at the 2000 CARICOM championship in Barbados, Belize placed fourth. Shortly thereafter, Belize moved to the Central American region and won the Central American Games championship in 2001.
</p><p>The team has failed to duplicate this success, most recently finishing with a 2–4 record in the 2006 COCABA championship. The team finished second in the 2009 COCABA tournament in Cancun, Mexico where it went 3–0 in group play. Belize won its opening match in the Centrobasquet Tournament, 2010, defeating Trinidad and Tobago, but lost badly to Mexico in a rematch of the COCABA final. A tough win over Cuba set Belize in position to advance, but they fell to Puerto Rico in their final match and failed to qualify.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Simone_Biles" title="Simone Biles">Simone Biles</a>, the winner of four gold medals in the 2016 Rio Summer Olympics is a dual citizen of the United States and of Belize,<sup id="cite_ref-Gazette_155-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Gazette-155">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup> which she considers her second home.<sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup> Biles is of Belizean-American descent.<sup id="cite_ref-Laymon_157-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Laymon-157">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="National_symbols">National symbols</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=48" title="Edit section: National symbols">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Prosthechea_cochleata_-_panoramio.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/Prosthechea_cochleata_-_panoramio.jpg/220px-Prosthechea_cochleata_-_panoramio.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/Prosthechea_cochleata_-_panoramio.jpg/330px-Prosthechea_cochleata_-_panoramio.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c5/Prosthechea_cochleata_-_panoramio.jpg/440px-Prosthechea_cochleata_-_panoramio.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3072" data-file-height="2304" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Prosthechea_cochleata_-_panoramio.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A black orchid flower (<i><a href="/wiki/Prosthechea_cochleata" title="Prosthechea cochleata">Prosthechea cochleata</a>)</i></div></div></div>
<p>The national flower of Belize is the black orchid (<i><a href="/wiki/Prosthechea_cochleata" title="Prosthechea cochleata">Prosthechea cochleata</a></i>, also known as <i>Encyclia cochleata</i>). The national tree is the <a href="/wiki/Mahogany" title="Mahogany">mahogany</a> tree (<i><a href="/wiki/Swietenia_macrophylla" title="Swietenia macrophylla">Swietenia macrophylla</a></i>), which inspired the national motto <i>Sub Umbra Floreo</i>, which means "Under the shade I flourish". The national ground-dwelling animal is the <a href="/wiki/Baird%27s_tapir" title="Baird&#39;s tapir">Baird's tapir</a> and the national bird is the keel-billed toucan (<i><a href="/wiki/Ramphastos_sulphuratus" class="mw-redirect" title="Ramphastos sulphuratus">Ramphastos sulphuratus</a></i>).<sup id="cite_ref-symbols_158-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-symbols-158">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="See_also">See also</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=49" title="Edit section: See also">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>

<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Explanatory_notes">Explanatory notes</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Belize&amp;action=edit&amp;section=50" title="Edit section: Explanatory notes">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1011085734">.mw-parser-output .reflist{font-size:90%;margin-bottom:0.5em;list-style-type:decimal}.mw-parser-output .reflist .references{font-size:100%;margin-bottom:0;list-style-type:inherit}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-2{column-width:30em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns-3{column-width:25em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns{margin-top:0.3em}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns ol{margin-top:0}.mw-parser-output .reflist-columns li{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-alpha{list-style-type:upper-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-upper-roman{list-style-type:upper-roman}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-alpha{list-style-type:lower-alpha}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-greek{list-style-type:lower-greek}.mw-parser-output .reflist-lower-roman{list-style-type:lower-roman}</style><div class="reflist reflist-lower-alpha">

<li id="cite_note-3"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-3">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">Percentages add up to more than 100% because respondents were able to identify more than one ethnic origin.</span>
</li>
<li id="cite_note-49"><span class="mw-cite-backlink"><b><a href="#cite_ref-49">^</a></b></span> <span class="reference-text">In April 2019, a media outlet showed video of Guatemalan president Jimmy Morales showing students how to draw Guatemala's map to include all of Belize, reflecting his country's claim.<sup id="cite_ref-48" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-48">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup></span>
</li>
</ol></div></div>
